\begin{split}\partial_{\rho}\eta(U)&=\int_{-1}^{1}g\left(\frac{m}{\rho}+z\rho^{\theta}\right)(1-z^{2})\,dz+\int_{-1}^{1}z\theta\rho^{\theta}g^{\prime}\left(\frac{m}{\rho}+z\rho^{\theta}\right)(1-z^{2})\,dz,\\
\partial_{m}\eta(U)&=\int_{-1}^{1}g^{\prime}\left(\frac{m}{\rho}+z\rho^{\theta}\right)(1-z^{2})\,dz.\end{split}

The uniform boundedness of
\eta
and
\nabla\eta
follow directly from
Lemma
3.1
, since the integrands are uniformly bounded in
L^{\infty}
. More specifically,

|\frac{m}{\rho}+z\rho^{\theta}|\leq C(\|U_{0}\|_{L^{\infty}},\gamma)
for
z\in[-1,1]
for each pair of functions

(\bar{\rho}_{\tau},\bar{u}_{\tau}),(\tilde{\rho}_{\tau},\tilde{u}_{\tau}),({\rho}_{\tau},{u}_{\tau})
. Since
g
is continuous,
\eta
,
\partial_{\rho}\eta
and
\partial_{m}\eta
are all bounded uniformly in
\epsilon
and
\tau
by some deterministic constant
C=C(\|U_{0}\|_{L^{\infty}},\gamma)
, independent of
T
.
∎

3.2.
Time regularity estimates

In this section, we are going to pass to the limit as
\tau\to 0
in the approximate solutions (
2.15
), and obtain a martingale solution to the parabolic approximation problem (
2.1
)–(
2.2
). To do this, we first derive the uniform-in-
\tau
time regularity of the approximate solution
{U}_{\tau}
in the following theorem.

Theorem 3.1

(Regularity of
{U}_{\tau}
)
.

Suppose
U_{\epsilon 0}\in H^{2}(0,1)
and
\rho_{\epsilon 0}\geq c
for some constant
c>0
, and the noise coefficient
\sigma_{\epsilon}
satisfies (
1.14
). Let
{U}_{\tau}
be defined in (
2.15
), and
\bar{U}_{\tau}
,
\tilde{U}_{\tau}
be defined in (
2.16
). Then

(3.5)

\mathbb{E}\|{U}_{\tau}\|^{2}_{C^{\alpha}(0,T;H^{1}(0,1))}\leq C,\;\;\text{ where }\alpha\in[0,\frac{1}{4}),

and

(3.6)

\mathbb{E}\|{U}_{\tau}\|^{2}_{C(0,T;H^{2}(0,1))}\leq C,\;\;\mathbb{E}\|\bar{U}_{\tau}\|^{2}_{L^{\infty}(0,T;H^{2}(0,1))}\leq C,\;\;\mathbb{E}\|\tilde{U}_{\tau}\|^{2}_{L^{\infty}(0,T;H^{2}(0,1))}\leq C,

where
C=C(\epsilon,T,\|U_{\epsilon 0}\|_{H^{2}},\|U_{\epsilon 0}\|_{L^{\infty}})
.

Proof.

To establish this result, we make some comments about the regularizing properties of the heat equation. Let
K_{\epsilon t}(x,y)
be the heat kernel associated with the heat equation
\partial_{t}z-\epsilon\partial_{x}^{2}z=0
on
[0,1]
with homogeneous Dirichlet boundary condition, which is given by

K_{\epsilon t}(x,y)=\sum_{n=-\infty}^{\infty}\frac{1}{\sqrt{4\pi\epsilon t}}\left(e^{-\frac{(x-y-2n)^{2}}{4\epsilon t}}-e^{-\frac{(x+y-2n)^{2}}{4\epsilon t}}\right),\;\;x,y\in[0,1].

Let
S_{\epsilon}(t)
be the corresponding heat semigroup.
A brief calculation gives that for every
y\in[0,1]
,

\|\partial_{t}^{k}\partial_{x}^{j}K_{\epsilon t}(\cdot,y)\|_{L^{m}_{x}}\leq C(m,k,j)\epsilon^{k}(\epsilon t)^{-\frac{1}{2}(\frac{1}{m^{\prime}})-\frac{j}{2}-k},

where
\frac{1}{m}+\frac{1}{m^{\prime}}=1
. Then we have the following regularizing property of
S_{\epsilon}
, thanks to the Young’s inequality for convolution:

(3.7)

\|\partial_{t}^{k}\partial_{x}^{j}S_{\epsilon}(t)\|_{L_{x}^{p}\to L_{x}^{q}}\leq\|\partial_{t}^{k}\partial_{x}^{j}K_{\epsilon t}(\cdot,y)\|_{L^{m}_{x}}\leq C(p,q,k,j)\epsilon^{k}(\epsilon t)^{-\frac{1}{2}(\frac{1}{p}-\frac{1}{q})-\frac{j}{2}-k}

for
k,j\in\mathbb{N}
,
1\leq p\leq q\leq+\infty
, and
1+\frac{1}{q}=\frac{1}{m}+\frac{1}{p}
. Note that we can use the heat semigroup
S_{\epsilon}(t)
to express
\bar{U}_{\tau}(t)
, as follows:

(3.8)

\bar{U}_{\tau}(t)=S_{\epsilon}(t-t_{n}){U}_{\tau}(t_{n})+\int_{t_{n}}^{t}\partial_{x}S_{\epsilon}(t-s^{\prime})F(\bar{U}_{\tau})\,ds^{\prime}-\int_{t_{n}}^{t}S_{\epsilon}(t-s^{\prime})G(\bar{U}_{\tau})\,ds^{\prime}.

Using this expression, we can now show the main statements of the theorem.

Step 1: Show that
{U}_{\tau}\in C^{\alpha}(0,T;L^{2}(0,1))
for
\alpha\in[0,1/4)
, almost surely
. To show this, by the Kolmogorov continuity criterion (see Theorem 3.5 and Theorem 5.22 in
[
25
]
), it suffices to show the following increment estimate for all
1\leq\beta<\infty
:

(3.9)

\mathbb{E}\|{U}_{\tau}(t)-{U}_{\tau}(s)\|^{\beta}_{L^{2}(0,1)}\leq C|t-s|^{\beta/4}\qquad\text{ for all }s,t\in[0,T],

for some constant
C:=C(\beta,\epsilon,T,\|U_{\epsilon 0}\|_{H^{2}},\|U_{\epsilon 0}\|_{L^{\infty}})
that is independent of
\tau
and
s,t\in[0,T]
.

First, we establish this inequality in the case where
t_{j}\leq s<t\leq t_{j+1}
. Note that:

(3.10)

\begin{split}&{U}_{\tau}(s)=\frac{t_{j+1}-s}{\tau}\mathbf{S}(s-t_{j}){U}_{\tau}(t_{j})+\frac{s-t_{j}}{\tau}\textbf{R}(s,t_{j})\textbf{S}(\tau){U}_{\tau}(t_{j})\\
=&\frac{t_{j+1}-s}{\tau}\left[S_{\epsilon}(s-t_{j}){U}_{\tau}(t_{j})+\int_{t_{j}}^{s}\partial_{x}S_{\epsilon}(s-s^{\prime})F(\bar{U}_{\tau}(s^{\prime}))-S_{\epsilon}(s-s^{\prime})G(\bar{U}_{\tau}(s^{\prime}))\,ds^{\prime}\right]\\
+&\frac{s-t_{j}}{\tau}\left[S_{\epsilon}(\tau){U}_{\tau}(t_{j})+\int_{t_{j}}^{t_{j+1}}\partial_{x}S_{\epsilon}(t_{j+1}-s^{\prime})F(\bar{U}_{\tau}(s^{\prime}))-S_{\epsilon}(t_{j+1}-s^{\prime})G(\bar{U}_{\tau})\,ds^{\prime}+\int_{t_{j}}^{s}\Phi_{\epsilon}(x,\tilde{U}_{\tau}(s^{\prime}))\,dW(s^{\prime})\right].\end{split}

Therefore, we obtain:

(3.11)

\begin{split}&{U}_{\tau}(t)-{U}_{\tau}(s)=\frac{t_{j+1}-t}{\tau}\left[S_{\epsilon}(t-t_{j}){U}_{\tau}(t_{j})-S_{\epsilon}(s-t_{j}){U}_{\tau}(t_{j})\right]-\frac{t-s}{\tau}S_{\epsilon}(s-t_{j}){U}_{\tau}(t_{j})+\frac{t-s}{\tau}S_{\epsilon}(\tau){U}_{\tau}(t_{j})\\
&+\frac{t_{j+1}-t}{\tau}\left[\int_{t_{j}}^{s}\left(\partial_{x}S_{\epsilon}(t-s^{\prime})-\partial_{x}S_{\epsilon}(s-s^{\prime})\right)F(\bar{U}_{\tau}(s^{\prime}))ds^{\prime}-\int_{t_{j}}^{s}\left(S_{\epsilon}(t-s^{\prime})-S_{\epsilon}(s-s^{\prime})\right)G(\bar{U}_{\tau}(s^{\prime}))ds^{\prime}\right]\\
&+\frac{t-s}{\tau}\int_{t_{j}}^{s}\partial_{x}S_{\epsilon}(s-s^{\prime})F(\bar{U}_{\tau}(s^{\prime}))-S_{\epsilon}(s-s^{\prime})G(\bar{U}_{\tau}(s^{\prime}))ds^{\prime}\\
&+\frac{t_{j+1}-t}{\tau}\int_{s}^{t}\partial_{x}S_{\epsilon}(t-s^{\prime})F(\bar{U}_{\tau}(s^{\prime}))-S_{\epsilon}(s-s^{\prime})G(\bar{U}_{\tau}(s^{\prime}))ds^{\prime}\\
&+\frac{t-s}{\tau}\int_{t_{j}}^{t_{j+1}}\partial_{x}S_{\epsilon}(t_{j+1}-s^{\prime})F(\bar{U}_{\tau}(s^{\prime}))-S_{\epsilon}(t_{j+1}-s^{\prime})G(\bar{U}_{\tau}(s^{\prime}))ds^{\prime}\\
&+\frac{t-t_{j}}{\tau}\int_{s}^{t}\Phi_{\epsilon}(x,\tilde{U}_{\tau}(s^{\prime}))\,dW(s^{\prime})+\frac{t-s}{\tau}\int_{t_{j}}^{s}\Phi_{\epsilon}(x,\tilde{U}_{\tau}(s^{\prime}))\,dW(s^{\prime}).\end{split}

Now, we estimate the
L^{2}
norm of each term on the right-hand side using the regularizing property of the heat semigroup (
3.7
), with the aim of proving (
3.9
) for
t_{j}\leq s\leq t\leq t_{j+1}
, for arbitrary
\beta>1
. Below, the symbol "
\lesssim
" means "
\leq C(j,k,p,q)
".

Term 1.
By (3.27) in Proposition 3.5 of
[
7
]
, we estimate:

(3.12)

\mathbb{E}\left\|S_{\epsilon}(t-t_{j}){U}_{\tau}(t_{j})-S_{\epsilon}(s-t_{j}){U}_{\tau}(t_{j})\right\|^{\beta}_{L^{2}(0,1)}\lesssim\epsilon^{-\beta/2}|t-s|^{\beta/2}\|\bar{U}_{\tau}(t_{j})\|^{\beta}_{H^{1}(0,1)}.

Term 2.
Next, we obtain that

\displaystyle\frac{t-s}{\tau}S_{\epsilon}(\tau){U}_{\tau}(t_{j})

\displaystyle-\frac{t-s}{\tau}S_{\epsilon}(s-t_{j}){U}_{\tau}(t_{j})

\displaystyle=\frac{t-s}{\tau}\Big[\Big(S_{\epsilon}(\tau)-S_{\epsilon}(0)\Big){U}_{\tau}(t_{j})-\Big(S_{\epsilon}(s-t_{j})-S_{\epsilon}(0)\Big){U}_{\tau}(t_{j})\Big]

\displaystyle=\frac{t-s}{\tau}\left(\int_{0}^{\tau}\partial_{t}S_{\epsilon}(r){U}_{\tau}(t_{j})dr+\int_{0}^{s-t_{j}}\partial_{t}S_{\epsilon}(r){U}_{\tau}(t_{j})dr\right).

Using (
3.7
) for
(j,k,p,q)=(0,1,\infty,2)
and the fact that
0\leq s-t_{j}\leq\tau
, we obtain

\displaystyle\left\|\frac{t-s}{\tau}S_{\epsilon}(\tau){U}_{\tau}(t_{j})-\frac{t-s}{\tau}S_{\epsilon}(s-t_{j}){U}_{\tau}(t_{j})\right\|_{L^{2}(0,1)}^{\beta}

\displaystyle\leq\left(\frac{2(t-s)}{\tau}\int_{0}^{\tau}r^{-3/4}\|{U}_{\tau}(t_{j})\|_{L^{\infty}(0,1)}dr\right)^{\beta}

\displaystyle\leq\left(\frac{2(t-s)}{\tau^{3/4}}\|{U}_{\tau}(t_{j})\|_{L^{\infty}(0,1)}\right)^{\beta}\lesssim(t-s)^{\beta/4}.

Term 3.
With the choice of
(j,k,p,q)=(1,1,\infty,2)
, we use (
3.7
) to estimate:

(3.13)

\begin{split}\mathbb{E}\Bigg\|\int_{t_{j}}^{s}\partial_{x}S_{\epsilon}(t-s^{\prime})&F(\bar{U}_{\tau}(s^{\prime}))-\partial_{x}S_{\epsilon}(s-s^{\prime})F(\bar{U}_{\tau}(s^{\prime}))\,ds^{\prime}\Bigg\|_{L^{2}(0,1)}^{\beta}\\
&\leq\mathbb{E}\left(\int_{t_{j}}^{s}\int_{s-s^{\prime}}^{t-s^{\prime}}\left\|\partial_{t}\partial_{x}S_{\epsilon}(t^{\prime})F(\bar{U}_{\tau}(s^{\prime}))\right\|_{L^{2}(0,1)}\,dt^{\prime}\,ds^{\prime}\right)^{\beta}\\
&\lesssim\mathbb{E}\left(\int_{t_{j}}^{s}\int_{s-s^{\prime}}^{t-s^{\prime}}(\epsilon)(\epsilon t^{\prime})^{-\frac{5}{4}}\|F(\bar{U}_{\tau}(s^{\prime}))\|_{L^{\infty}(0,1)}\,dt^{\prime}\,ds^{\prime}\right)^{\beta}\\
&\lesssim\mathbb{E}\left(\epsilon^{-1/4}\int_{t_{j}}^{s}\left(-(t-s^{\prime})^{-1/4}+(s-s^{\prime})^{-1/4}\right)\,ds^{\prime}\|F(\bar{U}_{\tau})\|_{L^{\infty}(0,T;L^{\infty}(0,1))}\right)^{\beta}\\
&\lesssim\mathbb{E}\left[\epsilon^{-1/4}\left((t-t_{j})^{3/4}-(s-t_{j})^{3/4}+(t-s)^{3/4}\right)\|F(\bar{U}_{\tau})\|_{L^{\infty}(0,1)}\right]^{\beta}\\
&\lesssim\epsilon^{-\beta/4}(t-s)^{3\beta/4}\mathbb{E}\|F(\bar{U}_{\tau})\|^{\beta}_{L^{\infty}(0,T;L^{\infty}(0,1))},\end{split}

where we recall that
t_{j}\leq s\leq t\leq t_{j+1}
, and where we use the following inequality for
p=3/4
:

(3.14)

0\leq\alpha^{p}-\beta^{p}\leq(\alpha-\beta)^{p},\qquad\text{ for }0\leq\alpha\leq\beta\text{ and }0<p\leq 1.

Similarly, with the choice of
(j,k,p,q)=(0,1,+\infty,2)
in (
3.7
), we have by (
3.14
) and calculation similar to the one above that:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Bigg\|\int_{t_{j}}^{s}S_{\epsilon}(t-s^{\prime})

\displaystyle G(\bar{U}_{\tau}(s^{\prime}))-S_{\epsilon}(s-s^{\prime})G(\bar{U}_{\tau}(s^{\prime}))\,ds^{\prime}\Bigg\|^{\beta}_{L^{2}(0,1)}

\displaystyle\lesssim\mathbb{E}\left[\epsilon^{1/4}\left((t-t_{j})^{5/4}-(s-t_{j})^{5/4}+(t-s)^{5/4}\right)\|G\|_{L^{\infty}(0,T;L^{\infty}(0,1))}\right]^{\beta}

(3.15)

\displaystyle\lesssim\epsilon(t-s)^{5\beta/4}\mathbb{E}\|G(\bar{U}_{\tau})\|_{L^{\infty}(0,T;L^{\infty}(0,1))}^{\beta}.

Terms 4-6 (terms involving
F(\bar{U}_{\tau})
).
With the choice of
(j,k,p,q)=(1,0,+\infty,2)
and by the fact that
t_{j}\leq s\leq t\leq t_{j+1}
, we have that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left\|\frac{t-s}{\tau}\int_{t_{j}}^{s}\partial_{x}S_{\epsilon}(s-s^{\prime})F(\bar{U}_{\tau})\,ds^{\prime}\right\|^{\beta}_{L^{2}(0,1)}

\displaystyle\lesssim\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{t-s}{\tau}\epsilon^{-1/4}\int_{t_{j}}^{s}(s-s^{\prime})^{-1/4}ds^{\prime}\|F(\bar{U}_{\tau})\|_{L^{\infty}(0,T;L^{\infty}(0,1))}\right]^{\beta}

\displaystyle\lesssim\Big[\epsilon^{-1}(t-s)\tau^{-1}(s-t_{j})^{3/4}\mathbb{E}\|F(\bar{U}_{\tau})\|_{L^{\infty}(0,T;L^{\infty}(0,1))}\Big]^{\beta}

(3.16)

\displaystyle\leq\epsilon^{-\beta/4}(t-s)^{3\beta/4}\mathbb{E}\|F(\bar{U}_{\tau})\|_{L^{\infty}(0,T;L^{\infty}(0,1))}^{\beta}.

Similarly, we compute that

\mathbb{E}\left\|\frac{t_{j+1}-t}{\tau}\int_{s}^{t}\partial_{x}S_{\epsilon}(t-s^{\prime})F(\bar{U}_{\tau}(s^{\prime}))ds^{\prime}+\frac{t-s}{\tau}\int_{t_{j}}^{t_{j+1}}\partial_{x}S_{\epsilon}(t_{j+1}-s^{\prime})F(\bar{U}_{\tau}(s^{\prime}))ds^{\prime}\right\|_{L^{2}(0,1)}^{\beta}\\
\lesssim\epsilon^{-\beta/4}\left[\frac{t_{j+1}-t}{\tau}(t-s)^{3/4}+\frac{t-s}{\tau}\tau^{3/4}\right]^{\beta}\mathbb{E}\|F(\bar{U}_{\tau})\|_{L^{\infty}(0,1)}^{\beta}\lesssim\epsilon^{-\beta/4}(t-s)^{3\beta/4}\mathbb{E}\|F(\bar{U}_{\tau})\|_{L^{\infty}(0,1)}^{\beta}.

Terms 4-6 (terms involving
G(\bar{U}_{\tau}))
.
With the choice of
(j,k,p,q)=(0,0,+\infty,2)
, we estimate for
t_{j}\leq s\leq t\leq t_{j+1}
as for the terms involving
F(\bar{U}_{\tau})
:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left\|\frac{t-s}{\tau}\int_{t_{j}}^{s}S_{\epsilon}(s-s^{\prime})G(\bar{U}_{\tau}(s^{\prime}))\,ds^{\prime}\right\|^{\beta}_{L^{2}(0,1)}

\displaystyle\lesssim\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{t-s}{\tau}\epsilon^{1/4}\int_{t_{j}}^{s}(s-s^{\prime})^{1/4}ds^{\prime}\|G(\bar{U}_{\tau})\|_{L^{\infty}(0,T;L^{\infty}(0,1))}\right]^{\beta}

\displaystyle\lesssim\epsilon^{\beta/4}(t-s)\tau^{-\beta/4}(s-t_{j})^{5\beta/4}\mathbb{E}\|G(\bar{U}_{\tau})\|_{L^{\infty}(0,T;L^{\infty}(0,1))}^{\beta}

(3.17)

\displaystyle\lesssim\epsilon^{-\beta/4}(t-s)^{\beta}\tau^{\beta/4}\mathbb{E}\|G(\bar{U}_{\tau})\|_{L^{\infty}(0,T;L^{\infty}(0,1))}^{\beta}.

We obtain a similar estimate for the remaining terms:

\mathbb{E}\left\|\frac{t_{j+1}-t}{\tau}\int_{s}^{t}S_{\epsilon}(s-s^{\prime})G(\bar{U}_{\tau}(s^{\prime}))ds^{\prime}+\frac{t-s}{\tau}\int_{t_{j}}^{t_{j+1}}S_{\epsilon}(t_{j+1}-s^{\prime})G(\bar{U}_{\tau}(s^{\prime}))ds^{\prime}\right\|_{L^{2}(0,1)}^{\beta}\\
\lesssim\epsilon^{\beta/4}(t-s)\tau^{\beta/4}\mathbb{E}\|G(\bar{U}_{\tau})\|^{\beta}_{L^{\infty}(0,T;L^{\infty}(0,1))}.

We refer to Proposition 3.5 in
[
7
]
for a more detailed calculation.

Estimate of the stochastic integrals.
Lastly, we estimate the Itô integrals in (
3.11
). Since the first component of the integral is
0
, it suffices to estimate the second component only:

(3.18)

\begin{split}\mathbb{E}\left(\left\|\frac{t-t_{j}}{\tau}\int_{s}^{t}\sigma_{\epsilon}(x,\tilde{U}_{\tau})\,dW(s^{\prime})\right\|_{L^{2}}\right)^{\beta}&=\left(\frac{t-t_{j}}{\tau}\right)^{\beta}\mathbb{E}\left(\int_{0}^{1}\left(\int_{s}^{t}\sigma_{\epsilon}(x,\tilde{U}_{\tau})\,dW(s^{\prime})\right)^{2}\,dx\right)^{\beta/2}\\
&=\left(\frac{t-t_{j}}{\tau}\right)^{\beta}\int_{0}^{1}\mathbb{E}\left(\int_{s}^{t}\sigma_{\epsilon}(x,\tilde{U}_{\tau})\,dW(s^{\prime})\right)^{\beta}\,dx\\
&\leq\left(\frac{t-t_{j}}{\tau}\right)^{\beta}\int_{0}^{1}\mathbb{E}\left(\int_{s}^{t}\sigma_{\epsilon}^{2}(x,\tilde{U}_{\tau})\,ds^{\prime}\right)^{\beta/2}\,dx\\
&\lesssim\left(\frac{t-t_{j}}{\tau}\right)^{\beta}(t-s)^{\beta/2}\leq(t-s)^{\beta/2},\end{split}

where in the first inequality we used the Burkholder-Davis-Gundy inequality; and the second inequality we used that
\sigma_{\epsilon}^{2}\leq A_{0}m^{2}
and
\tilde{m}_{\tau}
is uniformly bounded in the invariant region; in the rest of the inequality we used that
t_{j}\leq s\leq t\leq t_{j+1}
.
In a similar fashion, we have that

(3.19)

\mathbb{E}\left(\left\|\frac{t-s}{\tau}\int_{t_{j}}^{s}\sigma_{\epsilon}(x,\tilde{U}_{\tau})\,dW(s^{\prime})\right\|_{L^{2}(0,1)}\right)^{\beta}\lesssim\frac{(t-s)^{\beta}}{\tau^{\beta}}(t-s)^{\beta/2}\lesssim(t-s)^{\beta/2}.

Conclusion of Step 1.
Combining all of the estimates above, we have the increment estimate (
3.9
) for
\beta>1
, whenever
t_{j}\leq s\leq t\leq t_{j+1}
, namely whenever
s
and
t
are within the same subinterval
[t_{j},t_{j+1}]
. To establish the increment estimate (
3.9
) more generally for all
0\leq s\leq t\leq T
, it suffices by the triangle inequality to verify the estimate (
3.9
) whenever
s=t_{j+1}
and
t=t_{k}
for
j+1\leq k
. To do this, we express the term
{U}_{\tau}(t_{n})
for
n\geq j
, starting from initial condition
{U}_{\tau}(t_{j})
:

(3.20)

\begin{split}{U}_{\tau}(t_{n})=&S_{\epsilon}((n-j)\tau){U}_{\tau}(t_{j})+\sum_{k=j}^{n-1}S_{\epsilon}((n-k-1)\tau)\int_{t_{k}}^{t_{k+1}}\partial_{x}S_{\epsilon}(t_{k+1}-s^{\prime})F-S_{\epsilon}(t_{k+1}-s^{\prime})G\,ds^{\prime}\\
&+\sum_{k=j}^{n-1}S_{\epsilon}((n-k-1)\tau)\int_{t_{k}}^{t_{k+1}}\sigma_{\epsilon}(x,\tilde{U}_{\tau}(s^{\prime}))\,dW(s^{\prime}).\end{split}

Note that we have summation in this expression because each value
{U}_{\tau}(t_{i})
for
i=j,...,n-1
, is carried to the future steps as an initial condition by the heat semigroup. Next we use (
3.20
) (for
n=j+1
and
n=k
) to obtain

(3.21)

\begin{split}&{U}_{\tau}(t_{n})-{U}_{\tau}(t_{j+1})=\left[S_{\epsilon}((n-j)\tau)-S_{\epsilon}(\tau)\right]{U}_{\tau}(t_{j})\\
&+\left[S_{\epsilon}((n-j-1)\tau)-S_{\epsilon}(0)\right]\int_{t_{j}}^{t_{j+1}}\partial_{x}S_{\epsilon}(t_{j+1}-s^{\prime})F(\bar{U}_{\tau})-S_{\epsilon}(t_{j+1}-s^{\prime})G(\bar{U}_{\tau})\,ds^{\prime}\\
&+\sum_{k=j+1}^{n-1}S_{\epsilon}((n-k-1)\tau)\int_{t_{k}}^{t_{k+1}}\partial_{x}S_{\epsilon}(t_{k+1}-s^{\prime})F(\bar{U}_{\tau})-S_{\epsilon}(t_{k+1}-s^{\prime})G(\bar{U}_{\tau})\,ds^{\prime}\\
&+\left[S_{\epsilon}((n-j-1)\tau)-S_{\epsilon}(0)\right]\int_{t_{j}}^{t_{j+1}}\sigma_{\epsilon}(x,\tilde{U}_{\tau})\,dW(s^{\prime})+\sum_{k=j+1}^{n-1}S_{\epsilon}((n-k-1)\tau)\int_{t_{k}}^{t_{k+1}}\sigma_{\epsilon}(x,\tilde{U}_{\tau})\,dW(s^{\prime}).\end{split}

Note that the
L^{2}(0,1)
norm of all the individual integrals can be estimated in the same way using (
3.7
), as is done in (
3.13
)-(
3.2
). To deal with the effect of
S_{\epsilon}
applied to these integrals, we use again (
3.7
) with
(p,q,k,j)=(2,2,0,0)
to obtain a corresponding increment estimate (
3.9
) when
0\leq s=t_{j+1}\leq t=t_{k}\leq T
.

Therefore, we have proved that

(3.22)

\mathbb{E}\|{U}_{\tau}(t)-{U}_{\tau}(s)\|_{L^{2}(0,1)}^{\beta}\leq C|t-s|^{\beta/4},

where the constant
C=C(\beta,\epsilon,T,\|U_{\epsilon 0}\|_{H^{2}},\|U_{\epsilon 0}\|_{L^{\infty}})
, and is independent of
\tau
.
Finally, we can conclude through Kolmogorov’s continuity criterion (see Theorem 3.5 and Theorem 5.22 in
[
25
]
) that
{U}_{\tau}(t)
is sample continuous as a
L^{2}(0,1)
-valued random variable, and its sample path is
\alpha
-Hölder continuous, where
\alpha\in[0,\frac{1}{4})
, i.e.

(3.23)

\mathbb{E}\|{U}_{\tau}(t)\|^{2}_{C^{\alpha}(0,T;L^{2}(0,1))}\leq C(\alpha,\epsilon,T,\|U_{\epsilon 0}\|_{H^{2}},\|U_{\epsilon 0}\|_{L^{\infty}}).

Step 2: Show that
{U}_{\tau}\in C(0,T;H^{2}(0,1))
almost surely.
First, observe that for
t\in[0,t_{1}]
, we can show that
\partial_{x}\bar{U}_{\tau}(t)\in C(0,t_{1};H^{1}(0,1))
, and
\partial_{x}\tilde{U}_{\tau}(t)\in C(0,t_{1};H^{1}(0,1))
. For the proof we refer to Proposition 3.5 and Appendix B of
[
7
]
for detailed calculations. Using the definition of
{U}_{\tau}
in (
2.16
), we conclude that
{U}_{\tau}\in C(0,t_{1};H^{2}(0,1))
.

For later subintervals, namely for
t\in[t_{n},t_{n+1}]
, we observe that
\bar{U}_{\tau}(t)=\textbf{S}(t-t_{n}){U}_{\tau}(t_{n})
,
\tilde{U}_{\tau}(t)=\textbf{R}(t,t_{n})\textbf{S}(t_{n+1}-t_{n}){U}_{\tau}(t_{n})
, and

{U}_{\tau}(t_{n})\in H^{2}(0,1)
.
Hence, we can also show that
\bar{U}_{\tau},\tilde{U}_{\tau}\in C(t_{n},t_{n+1};H^{2}(0,1))
. Since
\lim_{t\to t_{n}^{-}}{U}_{\tau}(t)=\lim_{t\to t_{n}^{+}}{U}_{\tau}(t)
for any
n=1,...,N-1
, we can conclude that
{U}_{\tau}\in C(0,T;H^{2}(0,1))
. We also obtain
\bar{U}_{\tau},\tilde{U}_{\tau}\in L^{\infty}(0,T;H^{2}(0,1))
, since there is a potential jump discontinuity for each of
\bar{U}_{\tau}
and
\tilde{U}_{\tau}
, at each time
t=t_{n}
.

Step 3: Show that
\partial_{x}{U}_{\tau}\in C^{\alpha}(0,T;L^{2}(0,1))
almost surely.
By Step 2,
\partial_{x}{U}_{\tau}
is well-defined. Therefore we can differentiate (
3.10
) to obtain

(3.24)

\begin{split}\partial_{x}{U}_{\tau}(t)=&\frac{t_{j+1}-t}{\tau}\partial_{x}\textbf{S}(t-t_{j}){U}_{\tau}(t_{j})+\frac{t-t_{j}}{\tau}\partial_{x}\textbf{R}(t,t_{j})\textbf{S}(\tau){U}_{\tau}(t_{j}).\end{split}

Then, we implement the exact same process as in Step 1 and use Kolmogorov continuity criterion to obtain
\partial_{x}{U}_{\tau}\in C^{\alpha}(0,T;L^{2}(0,1))
almost surely.

∎

3.3.
Uniform entropy dissipation estimates

Recall that the approximate solutions satisfy an entropy balance equation (
3.31
) for the linear interpolant
{U}_{\tau}
. In this section, we will use the entropy balance equation for the approximate solutions to derive uniform bounds on the entropy dissipation (uniformly in
\tau
), which will be important for deriving uniform positivity estimates on the density in the next subsection, namely Section
3.4
. In particular, we show the following uniform estimate.

Proposition 3.2
.

There exists a constant
C=C(\epsilon,U_{\epsilon 0},\gamma,T)
that is independent of
\tau
, such that the approximate solution
(\bar{\rho}_{\tau},\bar{u}_{\tau})
to the deterministic subproblem, defined in (
2.16
) and (
2.17
), satisfies the following entropy dissipation estimate:

\mathbb{E}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{0}^{1}\bar{\rho}_{\tau}(\partial_{x}\bar{u}_{\tau})^{2}dxds\leq C.

Proof.

We start by noticing that (
3.32
), (
3.33
), and (
3.35
) imply the following entropy balance equation for the approximate solutions
\tilde{U}_{\tau}
and
\bar{U}_{\tau}
to the two subproblems, holding for every
T>0

\mathbb{P}
-almost surely, for all deterministic test functions
\varphi\in C^{2}_{c}((0,1))
:

(3.25)

\begin{split}&\int_{0}^{1}\eta(\tilde{U}_{\tau}(T,x))\varphi(x)\,dx\\
=&\int_{0}^{1}\eta(U_{\epsilon 0}(x))\varphi(x)\,dx+\int_{0}^{T}\int_{0}^{1}H(\bar{U}_{\tau})\partial_{x}\varphi\,dx\,ds-\alpha\int_{0}^{T}\int_{0}^{1}\bar{m}\partial_{m}\eta(\bar{U}_{\tau})\varphi(x)\,dx\,ds\\
&-\epsilon\int_{0}^{T}\int_{0}^{1}\langle\nabla^{2}\eta(\bar{U}_{\tau})\partial_{x}\bar{U}_{\tau},\partial_{x}\bar{U}_{\tau}\rangle\varphi(x)\,dx\,ds+\epsilon\int_{0}^{T}\int_{0}^{1}\eta(\bar{U}_{\tau})\partial_{x}^{2}\varphi\,dx\,ds\\
&+\frac{1}{2}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{0}^{1}\partial_{m}^{2}\eta(\tilde{U}_{\tau})\sigma_{\epsilon}^{2}(x,\tilde{U}_{\tau})\varphi(x)\,dx\,ds+\int_{0}^{T}\int_{0}^{1}\partial_{m}\eta(\tilde{U}_{\tau})\sigma_{\epsilon}(x,\tilde{U}_{\tau})\varphi(x)\,dx\,dW(s).\\
\end{split}

By substituting
\varphi(x)=1
,
\eta=\eta_{E}(\rho,m)=\frac{m^{2}}{2\rho}+p(\rho)
, and taking expectation on both sides of the equation we obtain:

(3.26)

\begin{split}\mathbb{E}\epsilon\int_{0}^{T}\int_{0}^{1}\langle\nabla^{2}\eta_{E}(\bar{U}_{\tau})\partial_{x}\bar{U}_{\tau},&\partial_{x}\bar{U}_{\tau}\rangle\,dx\,ds=\mathbb{E}\int_{0}^{1}\eta_{E}(U_{\epsilon 0}(x))\,dx-\mathbb{E}\int_{0}^{1}\eta_{E}(\tilde{U}_{\tau}(T,x))\,dx\\
&-\alpha\mathbb{E}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{0}^{1}\frac{\bar{m}_{\tau}^{2}}{\bar{\rho}_{\tau}}\,dx\,ds+\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{E}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{0}^{1}\frac{1}{\tilde{\rho}_{\tau}}\sigma_{\epsilon}^{2}(x,\tilde{U}_{\tau})\,dx\,ds.\end{split}

We express the Hessian term on the left hand-side as follows:

(3.27)

\langle\nabla^{2}\eta_{E}(\bar{U}_{\tau})\partial_{x}\bar{U}_{\tau},\partial_{x}\bar{U}_{\tau}\rangle=\kappa\gamma(\bar{\rho}_{\tau})^{\gamma-2}(\partial_{x}\bar{\rho}_{\tau})^{2}+\bar{\rho}_{\tau}(\partial_{x}\bar{u}_{\tau})^{2},

and notice that the term we want to estimate is the second term on the right-hand side.
For this purpose we estimate the terms on the right-hand side of (
3.26
) as follows.

First, using the Lipschitz continuity assumption on
\sigma_{\epsilon}
, namely
|\sigma_{\epsilon}(\cdot,\cdot,m)|^{2}\leq A_{0}m^{2}
, together with Lemma
3.1
, we obtain that the last term on the right hand-side of (
3.26
) can be estimated as:

(3.28)

\left|\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{E}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{0}^{1}\frac{1}{\tilde{\rho}_{\tau}}\sigma_{\epsilon}^{2}(x,\tilde{U}_{\tau})\,dx\,ds\right|\leq\left|\frac{A_{0}}{2}\mathbb{E}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{0}^{1}\frac{\tilde{m}_{\tau}^{2}}{\tilde{\rho}_{\tau}}\,dx\,ds\right|\leq C(U_{\epsilon 0},\gamma,T).

Similarly, using Lemma
3.1
and Lemma
3.2
, we can estimate the remaining terms to obtain:

(3.29)

\left|\mathbb{E}\int_{0}^{1}\eta_{E}(U_{\epsilon 0}(x))\,dx-\mathbb{E}\int_{0}^{1}\eta_{E}(\tilde{U}_{\tau}(T,x))\,dx\\
-\alpha\mathbb{E}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{0}^{1}\frac{\bar{m}_{\tau}^{2}}{\bar{\rho}_{\tau}}\,dx\,ds\right|\leq C(U_{\epsilon 0},\gamma,T).

Finally, by combining (
3.27
), (
3.28
) and (
3.29
), and by using the fact that
\kappa\gamma(\bar{\rho}_{\tau})^{\gamma-2}(\partial_{x}\bar{\rho}_{\tau})^{2}
is nonnegative, we get the desired estimate

\mathbb{E}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{0}^{1}\bar{\rho}_{\tau}(\partial_{x}\bar{u}_{\tau})^{2}\,dx\,ds\leq C(\epsilon,U_{\epsilon 0},\gamma,T)

where
C(\epsilon,U_{\epsilon 0},\gamma,T)
is independent of
\tau
.
∎

3.4.
Bounds on positivity of approximate density

In this section, we discuss the positivity properties of the approximate fluid densities
\{\rho_{\tau}\}_{\tau>0}
, which will be important for passing to the limit in the approximate entropy balance equation (
3.31
).
More precisely, we prove that there is a uniform in
\tau
lower bound on approximate densities that depends only on the lower bound of initial density, the entropy dissipation estimated in Proposition
3.2
, the
L^{\infty}
estimate of fluid velocity, and the length of the time interval
[0,T]
.

To show positivity of the approximate fluid densities, recall from the definition of the approximate solutions defined by our splitting scheme in (
2.15
) and (
2.16
), that density is updated only in the deterministic subproblem. In particular,
\bar{\rho}_{\tau}
is a weak solution to the regularized continuity equation

(3.30)

\partial_{t}\bar{\rho}_{\tau}+\partial_{x}(\bar{\rho}_{\tau}\bar{u}_{\tau})=\epsilon\Delta\bar{\rho}_{\tau}\quad\text{ on }[0,1],

with Neumann boundary conditions, see (
2.11
),
\bar{\mathbb{P}}
-almost surely.
Hence, we can show that
\bar{\rho}_{\tau}
is strictly positive,
\bar{\mathbb{P}}
-almost surely, by using the positivity properties of the regularized continuity equation.

For this purpose, we recall the following (deterministic) positivity result for the regularized continuity equation which can be proven with a slight modification of the proof for Theorem A.2 in
[
7
]
.

Proposition 3.3
.

Let
\rho_{0}\in H^{1}(0,1)
satisfy
\min_{x\in(0,1)}\rho_{0}(x)\geq c>0
for some positive constant
c
, and suppose that
\rho(x)
is a weak solution to

\partial_{t}\rho+\partial_{x}(\rho u)=\epsilon\Delta\rho,\qquad\text{ on }[0,1],

with Neumann boundary conditions, for some given function
u\in L^{\infty}((0,T)\times(0,1))
. Then, there exists a positive constant
c_{0}
, depending only on
c
,
T
,
\|u\|_{L^{\infty}((0,T)\times(0,1))}
, and

\int_{0}^{T}\int_{0}^{1}\rho(\partial_{x}u)^{2}dxdt,

such that
\rho(t,x)\geq c_{0}>0
for all
(t,x)\in[0,T]\times[0,1]
.

We will use this result and the previously derived uniform bounds on entropy dissipation and fluid velocity, to derive the following positivity result for the approximate densities. This result is similar to Theorem A.2 in
[
7
]
.

Proposition 3.4
.

Let the initial data
U_{\epsilon 0}=(\rho_{\epsilon 0},m_{\epsilon 0})
be defined by (
2.5
) with
\rho_{\epsilon 0}\geq c_{0}(\epsilon)>0
. Let
{U}_{\tau}=(\rho_{\tau},m_{\tau})
be the approximate solution of the Lie-Trotter splitting scheme defined in (
2.15
). Then there exists a random variable
c_{min}>0
depending only on
c_{0},T,\displaystyle\int_{Q_{T}}\bar{\rho}_{\tau}|\partial_{x}\bar{u}_{\tau}|^{2}\,dx\,dt
, and
\|\bar{u}_{\tau}\|_{L^{\infty}((0,T)\times(0,1))}
, such that
\bar{\rho}_{\tau}\geq c_{min}
and
{\rho}_{\tau}\geq c_{min}
almost surely.

Proof.

Since the approximate density is updated only during the deterministic subproblem, it is sufficient to prove that the densities
\{\bar{\rho}_{\tau}\}_{\tau>0}
, obtained by solving the deterministic subproblems (
2.11
), are uniformly bounded away from zero.
This will follow by
combining the positivity result on the fluid density in Proposition
3.3
with the uniform
L^{\infty}
bounds in Proposition
3.1
, the uniform bounds on the entropy dissipation in Proposition
3.2
, and the fact that the approximate solution
\bar{\rho}_{\tau}
satisfies the continuity equation (
3.30
). Namely, we obtain:

\bar{\rho}_{\tau}\geq c_{min}(\omega)>0,\qquad\forall\tau>0

for a constant
c_{min}(\omega)
depending only on the lower bound on
\rho_{\epsilon 0}
(see (
2.5
)), and the quantities
\displaystyle\int_{Q_{T}}\bar{\rho}_{\tau}|\partial_{x}\bar{u}_{\tau}|^{2}dxdt
and
\|\bar{u}_{\tau}\|_{L^{\infty}((0,T)\times(0,1))}
evaluated for the specific outcome
\omega\in\Omega
, which are almost surely finite by the estimates in Proposition
3.1
and Proposition
3.2
. ∎

3.5.
Approximate entropy equality

In this section, we prove an approximate entropy equality satisfied by the approximate solution
U_{\tau}
,
\bar{U}_{\tau}
, and
\tilde{U}_{\tau}
defined by the splitting scheme (
2.15
)-(
2.16
). We will then, in Section
3.7
, show that when passing
\tau\to 0
under this approximate entropy equality, we obtain the entropy equality (
2.10
) given in Theorem
2.1
.

Proposition 3.5
.

For every
n\in\{0,1,...,N-1\}
, and every
t\in(t_{n},t_{n+1}]
, the approximate solutions
{U}_{\tau}(t,x)
defined by (
2.15
), and
\bar{U}_{\tau}(t,x)
,
\tilde{U}_{\tau}(t,x)
defined by (
2.16
) satisfy the following entropy balance equation for every
\varphi\in C_{c}^{2}((0,1))
:

(3.31)

\begin{split}&\int_{0}^{1}\eta({U}_{\tau}(t))\varphi(x)\,dx=\int_{0}^{1}\eta(U_{\epsilon 0})\varphi(x)\,dx+\int_{0}^{t}\int_{0}^{1}H(\bar{U}_{\tau})\partial_{x}\varphi\,dx\,ds-\alpha\int_{0}^{t}\int_{0}^{1}\bar{m}\partial_{m}\eta(\bar{U}_{\tau})\varphi(x)\,dx\,ds\\
+&\epsilon\int_{0}^{t}\int_{0}^{1}\eta(\bar{U}_{\tau})\partial_{x}^{2}\varphi-\langle\nabla^{2}\eta(\bar{U}_{\tau})\partial_{x}\bar{U}_{\tau},\partial_{x}\bar{U}_{\tau}\rangle\varphi(x)\,dx\,ds\\
+&\frac{1}{2}\int_{0}^{t_{n}}\int_{0}^{1}\partial_{m}^{2}\eta(\tilde{U}_{\tau})\sigma_{\epsilon}^{2}(x,\tilde{U}_{\tau})\varphi(x)\,dx\,ds+\int_{0}^{t_{n}}\int_{0}^{1}\partial_{m}\eta(\tilde{U}_{\tau})\sigma_{\epsilon}(x,\tilde{U}_{\tau})\varphi(x)\,dx\,dW(s)\\
+&\frac{t-t_{n}}{\tau}\left(\int_{t}^{t_{n+1}}\int_{0}^{1}H(\bar{U}_{\tau})\partial_{x}\varphi-\alpha\bar{m}_{\tau}\partial_{m}\eta(\bar{U}_{\tau})\varphi(x)+\epsilon\eta(\bar{U}_{\tau})\partial_{x}^{2}\varphi-\epsilon\langle\nabla^{2}\eta(\bar{U}_{\tau})\partial_{x}\bar{U}_{\tau},\partial_{x}\bar{U}_{\tau}\rangle\varphi(x)\,dx\,ds\right)\\
+&\frac{t-t_{n}}{\tau}\left(\frac{1}{2}\int_{t_{n}}^{t}\int_{0}^{1}\partial_{m}^{2}\eta(\tilde{U}_{\tau})\sigma_{\epsilon}^{2}(x,\tilde{U}_{\tau})\varphi(x)\,dx\,ds+\int_{t_{n}}^{t}\int_{0}^{1}\partial_{m}\eta(\tilde{U}_{\tau})\sigma_{\epsilon}(x,\tilde{U}_{\tau})\varphi(x)\,dx\,dW(s)\right)\\
+&\int_{0}^{1}\mathrm{R}_{2}(\bar{U}_{\tau}-\tilde{U}_{\tau})\varphi(x)\,dx,\end{split}

where
\mathrm{R}_{2}
is given by:

\mathrm{R}_{2}(\bar{U}_{\tau}-\tilde{U}_{\tau})=\frac{1}{2}(\tilde{U}_{\tau}-\bar{U}_{\tau})^{T}\left((\frac{t-t_{n}}{\tau})^{2}\nabla^{2}\eta(\mathbf{\xi}_{1})-\frac{t-t_{n}}{\tau}\nabla^{2}\eta(\mathbf{\xi}_{2})\right)(\tilde{U}_{\tau}-\bar{U}_{\tau}),

for some
\xi_{1}=\xi_{1}(t,x),\xi_{2}=\xi_{2}(t,x)\in\mathbb{R}^{2}
on the line segment joining
\bar{U}_{\tau}(t,x)
and
\tilde{U}_{\tau}(t,x)
, for every
(t,x)\in Q_{T}
.

Proof.

For every
t\in(t_{n},t_{n+1}]
,
\bar{U}_{\tau}(t)
is the solution to the deterministic subproblem (
2.11
) and thus satisfies for every entropy-entropy flux pair
(\eta,H)
defined in (
1.8
), and every
\varphi\in C_{c}^{2}((0,1))
, the following entropy equality:

(3.32)

\begin{split}&\int_{0}^{1}\eta(\bar{U}_{\tau}(t))\varphi(x)\,dx+\epsilon\int_{t_{n}}^{t}\int_{0}^{1}\langle\nabla^{2}\eta(\bar{U}_{\tau})\partial_{x}\bar{U}_{\tau},\partial_{x}\bar{U}_{\tau}\rangle\varphi(x)\,dx\,ds=\int_{0}^{1}\eta({U}_{\tau}(t_{n}))\varphi(x)\,dx\\
&+\int_{t_{n}}^{t}\int_{0}^{1}H(\bar{U}_{\tau})\partial_{x}\varphi\,dx\,ds-\alpha\int_{t_{n}}^{t}\int_{0}^{1}\bar{m}\partial_{m}\eta(\bar{U}_{\tau})\varphi(x)\,dx\,ds+\epsilon\int_{t_{n}}^{t}\int_{0}^{1}\eta(\bar{U}_{\tau})\partial_{x}^{2}\varphi\,dx\,ds.\end{split}

By applying Itô’s formula with
U\mapsto\eta(U)
to the equation (
3.1
), we obtain that
\tilde{U}_{\tau}(t,x)
satisfies the following entropy equality, for every
\varphi\in C^{2}_{c}((0,1))
, and every
t\in(t_{n},t_{n+1}]
:

(3.33)

\begin{split}\int_{0}^{1}\eta(\tilde{U}_{\tau}(t))\varphi(x)\,dx=&\int_{0}^{1}\eta(\textbf{S}(\tau){U}_{\tau}(t_{n},x))\varphi(x)\,dx+\frac{1}{2}\int_{t_{n}}^{t}\int_{0}^{1}\partial_{m}^{2}\eta(\tilde{U}_{\tau})\sigma_{\epsilon}^{2}(x,\tilde{U}_{\tau})\varphi(x)\,dx\,ds\\
&+\int_{t_{n}}^{t}\int_{0}^{1}\partial_{m}\eta(\tilde{U}_{\tau})\sigma_{\epsilon}(x,\tilde{U}_{\tau})\varphi(x)\,dx\,dW(s),\end{split}

where
S
is the solution operator to the deterministic subproblem defined in (
2.11
).
To obtain the expression for
\eta({U}_{\tau})
, we calculate the Taylor expansion of
\eta({U}_{\tau})
and
\eta(\tilde{U}_{\tau})
centered around the point
\bar{U}_{\tau}
up to the second order:

(3.34)

\begin{split}\eta(U_{\tau})&=\eta\left(\frac{t_{n+1}-t}{\tau}\bar{U}_{\tau}+\frac{t-t_{n}}{\tau}\tilde{U}_{\tau}\right)=\eta\left(\bar{U}_{\tau}+\frac{t-t_{n}}{\tau}(\tilde{U}_{\tau}-\bar{U}_{\tau})\right)\\
&=\eta(\bar{U}_{\tau})+\frac{t-t_{n}}{\tau}\nabla\eta(\bar{U}_{\tau})\cdot(\tilde{U}_{\tau}-\bar{U}_{\tau})+\frac{1}{2}(\frac{t-t_{n}}{\tau})^{2}(\tilde{U}_{\tau}-\bar{U}_{\tau})^{T}\nabla^{2}\eta(\mathbf{\xi}_{1})(\tilde{U}_{\tau}-\bar{U}_{\tau}),\\
\eta(\tilde{U}_{\tau})&=\eta(\bar{U}_{\tau})+\nabla\eta(\bar{U}_{\tau})\cdot(\tilde{U}_{\tau}-\bar{U}_{\tau})+\frac{1}{2}(\tilde{U}_{\tau}-\bar{U}_{\tau})^{T}\nabla^{2}\eta(\mathbf{\xi}_{2})(\tilde{U}_{\tau}-\bar{U}_{\tau}),\end{split}

where
\xi_{1}=\xi_{1}(t,x),\xi_{2}=\xi_{2}(t,x)\in\mathbb{R}^{2}
are two points on the line segment joining
\bar{U}_{\tau}(t,x)
and
\tilde{U}_{\tau}(t,x)
, for every
(t,x)\in Q_{T}
. We remark here that
\eta
is twice differentiable in the state space
(0,\infty)\times\mathbb{R}
, because
\bar{\rho}_{\tau}
and
\tilde{\rho}_{\tau}
have strictly positive lower bounds,
\mathbb{P}
-almost surely by Proposition
3.4
. Therefore,
\nabla^{2}\eta(\xi)
is always well-defined for
\xi=\xi(t,x)\in\mathbb{R}^{2}
on the line segment joining
\bar{U}_{\tau}(t,x)
and
\tilde{U}_{\tau}(t,x)
.

We then multiply (
3.34
)
2
by
\frac{t-t_{n}}{\tau}
, subtract it from (
3.34
)
1
, and move
-\frac{t-t_{n}}{\tau}\eta(\tilde{U}_{\tau})
to the right hand side to obtain (
3.35
):

(3.35)

\begin{split}\eta({U}_{\tau})&=\eta\left(\frac{t_{n+1}-t}{\tau}\bar{U}_{\tau}+\frac{t-t_{n}}{\tau}\tilde{U}_{\tau}\right)\\
&=\frac{t_{n+1}-t}{\tau}\eta(\bar{U}_{\tau})+\frac{t-t_{n}}{\tau}\eta(\tilde{U}_{\tau})+\mathrm{R}_{2}(\bar{U}_{\tau}-\tilde{U}_{\tau}),\end{split}

where
\mathrm{R}_{2}(\bar{U}_{\tau}-\tilde{U}_{\tau})
is the second order Taylor remainder term given by

\mathrm{R}_{2}(\bar{U}_{\tau}-\tilde{U}_{\tau})=\frac{1}{2}(\tilde{U}_{\tau}-\bar{U}_{\tau})^{T}\left((\frac{t-t_{n}}{\tau})^{2}\nabla^{2}\eta(\mathbf{\xi}_{1})-\frac{t-t_{n}}{\tau}\nabla^{2}\eta(\mathbf{\xi}_{2})\right)(\tilde{U}_{\tau}-\bar{U}_{\tau}),

where
\xi_{1}=\xi_{1}(t,x),\xi_{2}=\xi_{2}(t,x)\in\mathbb{R}^{2}
are two points on the line segment joining
\bar{U}_{\tau}(t,x)
and
\tilde{U}_{\tau}(t,x)
, for every
(t,x)\in Q_{T}
. Note that the squared coefficient
(\frac{t-t_{n}}{\tau})^{2}
comes from the second order Taylor remainder term of the linear interpolant, whereas the term with
\frac{t-t_{n}}{\tau}
comes purely from the linear scaling of
\eta(\tilde{U}_{\tau})
.

We then substitute
\eta(\bar{U}_{\tau})
and
\eta(\tilde{U}_{\tau})
in (
3.35
) with the expressions from (
3.34
), and recursively express the initial term. After the substitution, we multiply the equation by
\varphi\in C^{2}_{c}((0,1))
and integrate over
[0,1]
, to finally obtain the entropy equality for
{U}_{\tau}
starting from
U_{\epsilon 0}
given by (
3.31
).
∎

3.6.
Difference estimates on approximate solutions

Before passing to the limit, we make one last observation about the approximate solutions. Namely, we recall from (
2.16
) that there are three definitions of approximate solutions:
\bar{U}_{\tau}
taking into account the deterministic subproblems only,
\tilde{U}_{\tau}
taking into account the stochastic subproblems only, and the linear interpolant
{U}_{\tau}
. These are all important to consider, since all forms of these solutions appear within the approximate entropy balance equation (
3.31
). However, we will prove in this section, that all three forms of the approximate solution are actually close together in an appropriate norm, and hence, when we pass to the limit after using the Skorohod representation theorem, they will all converge to the same limit as
\tau\to 0
.

Specifically, we will prove the following lemma, which provides pairwise estimates on the difference between the approximate solutions
\tilde{U}_{\tau}
,
\bar{U}_{\tau}
to the subproblems (
2.11
) and (
2.13
), and the approximate solution
{U}_{\tau}
, defined in (
2.15
).
These estimates will be useful later when we pass
\tau\to 0
and identify the limit.

Lemma 3.3
.

Fix
T>0
. Consider the solutions to the deterministic and stochastic subproblems
\bar{U}_{\tau}
and
\tilde{U}_{\tau}
defined in (
2.16
), and the approximate solution
{U}_{\tau}
defined by the interpolated Lie-Trotter splitting scheme (
2.15
). Then

(3.36)

\begin{split}\lim_{\tau\to 0}\mathbb{E}\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}\left\|\tilde{U}_{\tau}(t)-\bar{U}_{\tau}(t)\right\|_{L^{2}(0,1)}^{2}=0.\end{split}

The same result holds for the pairs
(U_{\tau},\bar{U}_{\tau})
and
(U_{\tau},\tilde{U}_{\tau})
.

Proof.

Consider any
t\in[0,T]
. By
the definition of
\tilde{U}_{\tau}
and
\bar{U}_{\tau}
in (
2.16
), we obtain that for
t=0
:

(3.37)

\tilde{U}_{\tau}(0)-\bar{U}_{\tau}(0)=\int_{0}^{t_{1}}\Big(\partial_{x}F(\bar{U}_{\tau})-G(\bar{U}_{\tau})+\epsilon\partial_{x}^{2}\bar{U}_{\tau}\Big)ds.

and more generally, for
t\in(t_{n},t_{n+1}]
:

(3.38)

\tilde{U}_{\tau}(t)-\bar{U}_{\tau}(t)=\int_{t}^{t_{n+1}}\Big(\partial_{x}F(\bar{U}_{\tau})-G(\bar{U}_{\tau})+\epsilon\partial_{x}^{2}\bar{U}_{\tau}\Big)ds+\int_{t_{n}}^{t}\Phi_{\epsilon}(x,\tilde{U}_{\tau})\,dW(s).

By using (
3.37
) and (
3.38
) along with the triangle inequality and the inequality
(A+B)^{2}\leq 2(A^{2}+B^{2})
, we obtain the following estimate:

(3.39)

\begin{split}&\mathbb{E}\sup_{0<t\leq T}\left\|\tilde{U}_{\tau}(t)-\bar{U}_{\tau}(t)\right\|_{L^{2}(0,1)}^{2}=\mathbb{E}\max_{n\in\{0,\dots,N-1\}}\sup_{t_{n}\leq t\leq t_{n+1}}\left\|\tilde{U}_{\tau}(t)-\bar{U}_{\tau}(t)\right\|_{L^{2}(0,1)}^{2}\\
=&\mathbb{E}\max_{n}\sup_{t_{n}\leq t\leq t_{n+1}}\int_{0}^{1}\left|\int_{t}^{t_{n+1}}\partial_{x}F(\bar{U}_{\tau})-G(\bar{U}_{\tau})+\epsilon\partial_{x}^{2}\bar{U}_{\tau}\,ds+\int_{t_{n}}^{t}\Phi_{\epsilon}(x,\tilde{U}_{\tau})\,dW(s)\right|^{2}\,dx\leq 2(I_{1}+I_{2}),\end{split}

where

I_{1}:=\tau\left(\mathbb{E}\max_{n}\sup_{t_{n}\leq t\leq t_{n+1}}\int_{t}^{t_{n+1}}\int_{0}^{1}\left|\partial_{x}F(\bar{U}_{\tau})-G(\bar{U}_{\tau})+\epsilon\partial_{x}^{2}\bar{U}_{\tau}\right|^{2}\,dx\,ds\right),

I_{2}:=\mathbb{E}\max_{n}\sup_{t_{n}\leq t\leq t_{n+1}}\int_{0}^{1}\left|\int_{t_{n}}^{t}\Phi_{\epsilon}(x,\tilde{U}_{\tau})\,dW(s)\right|^{2}\,dx.

Note that we used Hölder’s inequality and Fubini’s theorem to obtain the term
I_{1}
in the previous inequality (
3.39
).

Estimate of
I_{1}
.
We estimate the first integral by using Hölder’s inequality and Theorem

3.1
to deduce that:

(3.40)

\begin{split}&\mathbb{E}\max_{n}\sup_{t_{n}\leq t\leq t_{n+1}}\int_{t}^{t_{n+1}}\int_{0}^{1}\left|\partial_{x}F(\bar{U}_{\tau})-G(\bar{U}_{\tau})+\epsilon\partial_{x}^{2}\bar{U}_{\tau}\right|^{2}\,dx\,ds\\
\leq&\mathbb{E}\left\|\partial_{x}F(\bar{U}_{\tau})-G(\bar{U}_{\tau})+\epsilon\partial_{x}^{2}\bar{U}_{\tau}\right\|_{L^{\infty}(0,T;L^{2}(0,1))}^{2}|t_{n+1}-t|\leq C(U_{0},\gamma,\alpha,\epsilon,T)\tau.\end{split}

So we conclude that
I_{1}\leq C(U_{0},\gamma,\alpha,\epsilon,T)\tau^{2}
, and hence
I_{1}\to 0
as
\tau\to 0
.

Estimate of
I_{2}.
Next, we estimate the stochastic integral.
Consider
p>1
. We use the Burkholder-Davis-Gundy inequality, the estimate that
|\sigma_{\epsilon}(x,\tilde{U}_{\tau})|\leq\sqrt{A_{0}}|\tilde{m}_{\tau}|
from (
2.8
), and the uniform bounds in Proposition
3.1
on
\|\tilde{m}_{\tau}\|_{L^{\infty}(Q_{T})}
, to estimate that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left(\max_{n}\sup_{t_{n}\leq t\leq t_{n+1}}\int_{0}^{1}\left|\int_{t_{n}}^{t}\Phi_{\epsilon}(x,\tilde{U}_{\tau})dW(s)\right|^{2}dx\right)^{p}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[\max_{n}\sup_{t_{n}\leq t\leq t_{n+1}}\left(\int_{0}^{1}\left|\int_{t_{n}}^{t}\Phi_{\epsilon}(x,\tilde{U}_{\tau})dW(s)\right|^{2}dx\right)^{p}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{n=0}^{N-1}\mathbb{E}\left(\sup_{t_{n}\leq t\leq t_{n+1}}\left\|\int_{t_{n}}^{t}\Phi_{\epsilon}(x,\tilde{U}_{\tau})dW(s)\right\|^{2p}_{L^{2}(0,1)}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{n=0}^{N-1}\mathbb{E}\left(\int_{t_{n}}^{t_{n+1}}\|\sigma_{\epsilon}(x,\tilde{U}_{\tau}(s))\|^{2}_{L^{2}(0,1)}ds\right)^{p}

\displaystyle\leq(A_{0})^{p}\sum_{n=0}^{N-1}\mathbb{E}\left(\int_{t_{n}}^{t_{n+1}}\|\tilde{m}_{\tau}\|_{L^{2}(0,1)}^{2}ds\right)^{p}

\displaystyle\leq(A_{0})^{p}C\sum_{n=0}^{N-1}\tau^{p}\leq(A_{0})^{p}C(N\tau^{p})

(3.41)

\displaystyle=(A_{0})^{p}CT\tau^{p-1}.

Hence, by using the fact that
\mathbb{E}|X|\leq\Big[\mathbb{E}(|X|^{p})\Big]^{1/p}
, we have that
I_{2}\to 0
, as
\tau\to 0
.

Conclusion.
Combining the estimates of
I_{1}
and
I_{2}
in (
3.39
), we obtain that

(3.42)

\lim_{\tau\to 0}\mathbb{E}\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}\left\|\tilde{U}_{\tau}(t)-\bar{U}_{\tau}(t)\right\|_{L^{2}(0,1)}^{2}=0.

To see that the same result holds for the pairs
(U_{\tau},\bar{U}_{\tau})
and
(U_{\tau},\tilde{U}_{\tau})
, we simply observe that

|{U}_{\tau}(t)-\bar{U}_{\tau}(t)|=\frac{t-t_{n}}{\tau}|\tilde{U}_{\tau}(t)-\bar{U}_{\tau}(t)|\leq|\tilde{U}_{\tau}(t)-\bar{U}_{\tau}(t)|,

|{U}_{\tau}(t)-\tilde{U}_{\tau}(t)|=\frac{t_{n+1}-t}{\tau}|\tilde{U}_{\tau}(t)-\bar{U}_{\tau}(t)|\leq|\tilde{U}_{\tau}(t)-\bar{U}_{\tau}(t)|.

∎

Next we give a corresponding difference estimate for the gradient of the three different approximate solutions,
\bar{U}_{\tau}
,
\tilde{U}_{\tau}
, and
{U}_{\tau}
.

Lemma 3.4
.

Fix
T>0
. Let
{U}_{\tau}
,
\bar{U}_{\tau}
, and
\tilde{U}_{\tau}
be defined in (
2.15
) and (
2.16
). Then

(3.43)

\begin{split}\lim_{\tau\to 0}\mathbb{E}\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}\|\partial_{x}\bar{U}_{\tau}(t)-\partial_{x}\tilde{U}_{\tau}(t)\|^{2}_{L^{2}(0,1)}=0.\end{split}

Moreover, the same result holds for the pairs
(U_{\tau},\bar{U}_{\tau})
and
(U_{\tau},\tilde{U}_{\tau})
.

Proof.

We start by recalling the definition of
\bar{U}_{\tau}(t)
in (
2.16
) and that
\bar{U}_{\tau}(0)=U_{\epsilon 0}
. When
t\in(t_{n},t_{n+1}]
,
\bar{U}_{\tau}(t)
can be expressed using the solution operator
\mathbf{S}
(see (
2.12
)) and the heat semigroup
S_{\epsilon}
(introduced in Theorem
3.1
) in the following way:

(3.44)

\bar{U}_{\tau}(t)=\mathbf{S}(t-t_{n})U_{\tau}(t_{n})=S_{\epsilon}(t-t_{n})U_{\tau}(t_{n})+\int_{t_{n}}^{t}S_{\epsilon}(t-r)\partial_{x}F(\bar{U}_{\tau}(r))\,dr-\int_{t_{n}}^{t}S_{\epsilon}(t-r)G(\bar{U}_{\tau}(r))\,dr.

By the definition of the regularized initial condition
U_{\epsilon 0}
(see (
2.1
)), the spatial derivative
\partial_{x}\bar{U}_{\tau}(0)=\partial_{x}U_{\tau}(0)=\partial_{x}U_{\epsilon 0}
is well defined.
By Theorem
3.1
,
\bar{U}_{\tau}\in L^{\infty}(0,T;H^{2}(0,1))
and
U_{\tau}\in C(0,T;H^{2}(0,1))
,
\mathbb{P}
-almost surely. Therefore, we can also then differentiate with respect to
x
on both sides of (
3.44
) to obtain by properties of convolution with respect to differentiation:

(3.45)

\partial_{x}\bar{U}_{\tau}(t)=\begin{cases}\partial_{x}U_{\tau}(0)&t=0\\
\begin{split}\partial_{x}\left(\mathbf{S}(t-t_{n})U_{\tau}(t_{n})\right)&=S_{\epsilon}(t-t_{n})\partial_{x}{U_{\tau}(t_{n})}+\int_{t_{n}}^{t}\partial_{x}S_{\epsilon}(t-r)\partial_{x}F(\bar{U}_{\tau}(r))\,dr\\
&-\int_{t_{n}}^{t}\partial_{x}S_{\epsilon}(t-r)G(\bar{U}_{\tau}(r))\,dr\end{split}&t\in(t_{n},t_{n+1}].\end{cases}

Similarly, we recall the definition of
\tilde{U}_{\tau}
in (
2.16
)
2
and differentiate (
2.16
)
2
on both sides with respect to
x
to obtain:

(3.46)

\partial_{x}\tilde{U}_{\tau}(t)=\begin{cases}\partial_{x}(\mathbf{S}(\tau){U}_{\tau}(0))&t=0\\
\partial_{x}(\mathbf{S}(\tau){U}_{\tau}(t_{n}))+\int_{t_{n}}^{t}\partial_{x}\Phi_{\epsilon}(x,\tilde{U}_{\tau})\,dW(r)&t\in(t_{n},t_{n+1}].\end{cases}

After replacing
\partial_{x}(\textbf{S}(\tau)U_{\tau}(0))
and
\partial_{x}(\textbf{S}(\tau)U_{\tau}(t_{n}))
with the expression involving the heat semigroup in (
3.45
), and subtracting (
3.45
) from (
3.46
), we have that
for
t=0
:

(3.47)

\begin{split}&\mathbb{E}\|\partial_{x}\bar{U}_{\tau}(0)-\partial_{x}\tilde{U}_{\tau}(0)\|_{L^{2}(0,1)}^{2}\\
=&\mathbb{E}\int_{0}^{1}\left|[S_{\epsilon}(\tau)-S_{\epsilon}(0)]\partial_{x}U_{\tau}(0)+\int_{0}^{t_{1}}S_{\epsilon}(t_{1}-r)\partial_{x}^{2}F(\bar{U}_{\tau}(r))-S_{\epsilon}(t_{1}-r)\partial_{x}G(\bar{U}_{\tau}(r))\,dr\right|^{2}\,dx.\end{split}

For
t\in(t_{n},t_{n+1}]
, we apply the triangle inequality and obtain

(3.48)

\begin{split}&\mathbb{E}\sup_{0<t\leq T}\left\|\partial_{x}\bar{U}_{\tau}(t)-\partial_{x}\tilde{U}_{\tau}(t)\right\|^{2}_{L^{2}(0,1)}\leq\mathbb{E}\max_{n}\sup_{t_{n}<t\leq t_{n+1}}\int_{0}^{1}\left|\left[S_{\epsilon}(\tau)-S_{\epsilon}(t-t_{n})\right]\partial_{x}{U}_{\tau}(t_{n})\right|^{2}\,dx\\
&+\mathbb{E}\max_{n}\int_{0}^{1}\left|\int_{t_{n}}^{t_{n+1}}\partial_{x}S_{\epsilon}(t_{n+1}-r)\partial_{x}F(\bar{U}_{\tau})-\partial_{x}S_{\epsilon}(t_{n+1}-r)G(\bar{U}_{\tau})\,dr\right|^{2}\,dx\\
&+\mathbb{E}\max_{n}\sup_{t_{n}<t\leq t_{n+1}}\int_{0}^{1}\left|\int_{t_{n}}^{t}\partial_{x}S_{\epsilon}(t-r)\partial_{x}F(\bar{U}_{\tau})-\partial_{x}S_{\epsilon}(t-r)G(\bar{U}_{\tau})\,dr\right|^{2}\,dx\\
&+\mathbb{E}\max_{n}\sup_{t_{n}<t\leq t_{n+1}}\int_{0}^{1}\left|\int_{t_{n}}^{t}\partial_{x}\Phi_{\epsilon}(x,\tilde{U}_{\tau})\,dW(r)\right|^{2}\,dx=:I_{0}+I_{1}+I_{2}+I_{3}.\end{split}

We then only demonstrate the estimates for the
t\in(t_{n},t_{n+1}]
case below, since a similar argument can be applied to the more specific case of
t=0
. For this purpose, we recall the estimate on the heat semigroup from (
3.7
):

\|\partial_{t}^{k}\partial_{x}^{j}S_{\epsilon}(t)\|_{L_{x}^{p}\to L_{x}^{q}}\leq\|\partial_{t}^{k}\partial_{x}^{j}K_{\epsilon t}(\cdot,y)\|_{L^{m}_{x}}\leq C(p,q,k,j)\epsilon^{k}(\epsilon t)^{-\frac{1}{2}(\frac{1}{p}-\frac{1}{q})-\frac{j}{2}-k}

for
k,j\in\mathbb{N}
,
1\leq p\leq q\leq+\infty
, and
1+\frac{1}{q}=\frac{1}{m}+\frac{1}{p}
.

We first write the integrand in
I_{0}
as

\left[S_{\epsilon}(\tau)-S_{\epsilon}(t-t_{n})\right]\partial_{x}{U}_{\tau}(t_{n})=\int_{t-t_{n}}^{\tau}\partial_{r}S_{\epsilon}(r)\partial_{x}{U}_{\tau}(t_{n})\,dr.

Using (
3.7
) for
(j,k,p,q)=(0,1,\infty,2)
, the fact that
\partial_{x}{U}_{\tau}(t_{n})\in H^{1}(0,1)\hookrightarrow L^{\infty}(0,1)
by Theorem
3.1
, and the fact that
|\tau-(t-t_{n})|\leq\tau
, we have that

(3.49)

I_{0}\leq\mathbb{E}\max_{n}\sup_{t_{n}<t\leq t_{n+1}}\left(\int_{t-t_{n}}^{\tau}r^{-3/4}\|\partial_{x}\bar{U}_{\tau}(t_{n})\|_{L^{\infty}(0,1)}\,dr\right)^{2}\leq C\tau^{1/2},

for some constant
C=C(U_{0},\epsilon,\gamma,\alpha,T)
. We estimate
I_{1}
in a similar manner as we estimate the deterministic integral in Lemma
3.3
. By Hölder’s inequality and (
3.7
) for
(j,k,p,q)=(1,0,\infty,2)
, we have that:

(3.50)

\begin{split}I_{1}&\leq\tau\cdot\mathbb{E}\max_{n}\int_{t_{n}}^{t_{n+1}}\int_{0}^{1}\left|\partial_{x}S_{\epsilon}(t_{n+1}-r)\partial_{x}F(\bar{U}_{\tau})-\partial_{x}S_{\epsilon}(t_{n+1}-r)G(\bar{U}_{\tau})\right|^{2}\,dx\,dr\\
&\leq\tau\cdot\mathbb{E}\max_{n}\int_{t_{n}}^{t_{n+1}}\|\partial_{x}S_{\epsilon}(t_{n+1}-\cdot)\partial_{x}F(\bar{U}_{\tau})-\partial_{x}S_{\epsilon}(t_{n+1}-\cdot)G(\bar{U}_{\tau})\|_{L^{2}(0,1)}^{2}dr\\
&\leq C\tau\cdot\mathbb{E}\max_{n}\int_{t_{n}}^{t_{n+1}}(t_{n+1}-r)^{-1/2}\Big(\|\partial_{x}F(\bar{U}_{\tau})\|_{L^{\infty}(0,1)}+\|G(\bar{U}_{\tau})\|_{L^{\infty}(0,1)}\Big)^{2}dr\\
&\leq C\tau^{3/2}\cdot\mathbb{E}\Big(1+\|\partial_{x}\bar{U}_{\tau}\|_{L^{\infty}((0,T)\times(0,1))}^{2}\Big)\\
&\leq C\tau^{3/2}\cdot\mathbb{E}\Big(1+\|\bar{U}_{\tau}\|_{L^{\infty}(0,T;H^{2}(0,1))}^{2}\Big)\leq C\tau^{3/2}.\end{split}

Here, we used Theorem
3.1
, which implies that
\mathbb{E}\|\bar{U}_{\tau}\|_{L^{\infty}(0,T;H^{2}(0,1))}^{2}\leq C
, and we used the
L^{\infty}
boundedness in Proposition
3.1
to conclude that
\|\partial_{x}F(\bar{U}_{\tau})\|_{L^{\infty}(0,1)}\leq C\|\partial_{x}\bar{U}_{\tau}\|_{L^{\infty}(0,1)}
and
\|G(\bar{U}_{\tau})\|_{L^{\infty}(0,1)}\leq C
. Similarly,
I_{2}\leq C\tau^{3/2}
.
For the stochastic integral,
I_{3}
, we estimate it in a similar fashion as we did with the stochastic integral in Lemma
3.3
and conclude that

(3.51)

\begin{split}\lim_{\tau\to 0}I_{3}=\lim_{\tau\to 0}\mathbb{E}\max_{n}\sup_{t_{n}<t\leq t_{n+1}}\int_{0}^{1}\left|\int_{t_{n}}^{t}\partial_{x}\Phi_{\epsilon}(x,\tilde{U}_{\tau})\,dW(r)\right|^{2}\,dx=0.\end{split}

Together we have obtained that

\lim_{\tau\to 0}\mathbb{E}\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}\int_{0}^{1}|\partial_{x}\bar{U}_{\tau}(t)-\partial_{x}\tilde{U}_{\tau}(t)|^{2}\,dx=0.

To show that the same result applies to the pairs
(\partial_{x}U_{\tau},\partial_{x}\bar{U}_{\tau})
and
(\partial_{x}U_{\tau},\partial_{x}\tilde{U}_{\tau})
, we simply note that
\partial_{x}(\bar{U}_{\tau}(t)-{U}_{\tau}(t))=\frac{t-t_{n}}{\tau}\partial_{x}(\tilde{U}_{\tau}(t)-\bar{U}_{\tau}(t))
, and
\partial_{x}(\tilde{U}_{\tau}(t)-{U}_{\tau}(t))=\frac{t_{n+1}-t}{\tau}\partial_{x}(\tilde{U}_{\tau}(t)-\bar{U}_{\tau}(t))
. Therefore,

|\partial_{x}({U}_{\tau}(t)-\bar{U}_{\tau}(t))|\leq|\partial_{x}(\tilde{U}_{\tau}-\bar{U}_{\tau})|,\qquad|\partial_{x}({U}_{\tau}(t)-\tilde{U}_{\tau}(t))|\leq|\partial_{x}(\tilde{U}_{\tau}-\bar{U}_{\tau})|.

We then conclude the proof of this lemma.
∎

3.7.
Skorohod representation theorem

We will now combine all of the uniform bounds on
U_{\tau}
independent of
\tau
, which are defined in Section
3.1
–Section
3.6
, in order to pass to the limit in the approximate (random-in-time) solutions
U_{\tau}
as
\tau\to 0
. To do this, we will use a classical
stochastic compactness argument
, which involves examining the laws of the approximate solutions in
\tau
, and then showing that the laws converge weakly (along a subsequence in
\tau
) to a limiting probability measure as
\tau\to 0
. This will be accomplished by showing that the laws of the approximate solutions are uniformly
tight
in
\tau
. Once we have weak convergence of the laws of the approximate solutions, we can then use the classical Skorohod representation theorem to obtain almost sure convergence of the actual random variables defining the approximate solutions, but at the expense of transferring to a different probability space. We can then transfer this almost sure convergence of the approximate solutions back to the
original probability space
by using a strong pathwise uniqueness property of the approximate system (
2.1
) via a standard Gyöngy-Krylov argument, as shown in Section
3.8
. We carry out this stochastic compactness procedure below.

The approximate
\tau
-level quantities that we need to keep track of are
(U_{\tau},\bar{\rho}_{\tau}^{1/2}\partial_{x}\bar{u}_{\tau},W)
. We remark that we keep track of
\bar{\rho}_{\tau}^{1/2}\partial_{x}\bar{u}_{\tau}
in order to obtain uniform control of the approximate densities away from vacuum. We recall the definition of the approximate fluid density
\bar{\rho}_{\tau}
from the definition of
\bar{U}_{\tau}:=(\bar{\rho}_{\tau},\bar{m}_{\tau})
in (
2.16
), and we recall that
\bar{u}_{\tau}:=\bar{m}_{\tau}/\bar{\rho}_{\tau}
is the approximate fluid velocity, which is well-defined by the lower bound in Proposition
3.4
. For these approximate quantities
(U_{\tau},\bar{\rho}_{\tau}^{1/2}\partial_{x}\bar{u}_{\tau},W)
, we define the following path spaces:

\mathcal{X}_{U}:=C(0,T;H^{1}(0,1)),\quad\mathcal{X}_{\rho^{1/2}\partial_{x}u}:=(L^{2}((0,T)\times(0,1)),w),\quad\mathcal{X}_{W}:=C(0,T;\mathbb{R}),

and the combined path space:

(3.52)

\mathcal{X}:=\mathcal{X}_{U}\times\mathcal{X}_{\rho^{1/2}\partial_{x}{u}}\times\mathcal{X}_{W}.

Here,
(L^{2}((0,T)\times(0,1)),w)
refers to the space
L^{2}((0,T)\times(0,1))
with the topology of weak convergence. We denote the joint law of the approximate quantities in the path space
\mathcal{X}
by
\mu_{\tau}
, namely:

\mu_{\tau}:=\text{Law}_{\mathcal{X}}(U_{\tau},\bar{\rho}_{\tau}^{1/2}\partial_{x}\bar{u}_{\tau},W).

To show that the probability measures
\mu_{\tau}
on
\mathcal{X}
converge weakly along a subsequence in
\tau
, we are going to use the regularity of
{U}_{\tau}
(Theorem
3.1
) and
\bar{\rho}_{\tau}^{1/2}\partial_{x}\bar{u}_{\tau}
(Proposition
3.2
) to deduce uniform tightness of the joint laws of
\{{U}_{\tau}\}_{\tau}
,
\{\bar{\rho}_{\tau}^{1/2}\partial_{x}\bar{u}_{\tau}\}_{\tau}
, and
W
in the space
\mathcal{X}
.

Theorem 3.2

(Tightness of law)
.

Let
\{{\mu}_{\tau}\}_{\tau}
be the joint laws of the random variables
({U}_{\tau},\bar{\rho}_{\tau}^{1/2}\partial_{x}\bar{u}_{\tau},W)
. Then
\{\mu_{\tau}\}_{\tau}
is uniformly tight in the path space
\mathcal{X}
.

Proof.

Tightness of laws of
\{{U}_{\tau}\}_{\tau}
.

Define the following subset of
\mathcal{X}_{U}
:

K_{M}:=\{U\in C(0,T;H^{1}(0,1)):\|U\|_{C^{\alpha}((0,T);H^{1}(0,1))}+\|U\|_{C(0,T;H^{2}(0,1))}>M\}.

By Arzela-Ascoli,
C^{\alpha}(0,T;H^{1}(0,1))\cap C(0,T;H^{2}(0,1))
compactly embeds in
C(0,T;H^{1}(0,1))
, and hence, to show the tightness of the laws of
\{U_{\tau}\}_{\tau}
in
\mathcal{X}_{U}
, we observe that:

(3.53)

\begin{split}\mathbb{P}\left({U}_{\tau}\notin K_{M}\right)&=\mathbb{P}\left(\|{U}_{\tau}\|_{C^{\alpha}((0,T);H^{1}(0,1))}+\|{U}_{\tau}\|_{C(0,T;H^{2}(0,1))}>M\right)\\
&\leq\frac{\mathbb{E}\|{U}_{\tau}\|^{2}_{C^{\alpha}((0,T);H^{1}(0,1))}+\mathbb{E}\|{U}_{\tau}\|^{2}_{C(0,T;H^{2}(0,1))}}{M^{2}}\leq\frac{C}{M^{2}},\end{split}

where we use Markov’s inequality, and the estimates in Theorem
3.1
. Therefore we have shown that for every
\epsilon>0
, we can find a compact set
K\subset\subset C(0,T;H^{1}(0,1))
such that
\mu_{{U}_{\tau}}({U}_{\tau}\notin K)<\epsilon
, which implies that the laws of
\{U_{\tau}\}_{\tau}
form a tight family of measures on
\mathcal{X}_{U}=C(0,T;H^{1}(0,1))
.

Tightness of laws of
\{\bar{\rho}_{\tau}^{1/2}\partial_{x}\bar{u}_{\tau}\}_{\tau}
.

By Proposition
3.2
,

\mathbb{E}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{0}^{1}\bar{\rho}_{\tau}(\partial_{x}\bar{u}_{\tau})^{2}dxdt\leq C.

for a constant
C
that is independent of
\tau
. This shows the tightness of laws of
\{\bar{\rho}_{\tau}^{1/2}\partial_{x}\bar{u}_{\tau}\}_{\tau}
in
(L^{2}((0,T)\times(0,1)),w)
with the weak topology, since any closed ball in
L^{2}((0,T)\times(0,1))
is weakly compact by the Banach-Alaoglu theorem.

Conclusion of the proof.
Finally, the law of the Brownian motions are trivially tight in
C(0,T;\mathbb{R})
, since the Brownian motion is the same, independently of
\tau
. Therefore, the collection of joint laws,
\{\mu_{\tau}\}_{\tau}
is uniformly tight in the phase space
\mathcal{X}
, defined in (
3.52
).

∎

From this result on the tightness of laws, we can then apply the Skorohod Representation Theorem (see for example pg. 70 of
[
9
]
) to obtain an almost surely convergent sequence of random variables defined on a new probability space, which is stated in Theorem
3.3
below. Though this requires transferring to a different probability space, the almost sure convergence that we obtain on the new probability space allows us to pass to the limit in the
\tau
-level entropy equality as
\tau\to 0
.

Theorem 3.3

(Passage to the limit)
.

Suppose the initial condition
U_{\epsilon 0}
is defined by (
2.5
), and the noise coefficient
\sigma_{\epsilon}
satisfies (
1.14
). Let
{U}_{\tau}
be defined as in (
2.16
), then there exists a sequence of
\mathcal{X}
-valued random variables
({U}^{\sharp}_{\tau},\ell^{\sharp}_{\tau},W^{\sharp}_{\tau})_{\tau}
, and a triple of
\mathcal{X}
-valued random variables
(U_{\epsilon},\ell_{\epsilon},W^{\sharp})
on the probability space
(\tilde{\Omega},\tilde{\mathcal{F}},\tilde{\mathbb{P}})
, such that

•

({U}^{\sharp}_{\tau},\ell^{\sharp}_{\tau},W^{\sharp}_{\tau})\to(U_{\epsilon},\ell_{\epsilon},W^{\sharp})
as
\tau\to 0
, in the topology of
\mathcal{X}_{U}\times\mathcal{X}_{\rho^{1/2}\partial_{x}u}\times\mathcal{X}_{W}
,
\tilde{\mathbb{P}}
-almost surely.

•

({U}_{\tau},\bar{\rho}_{\tau}^{1/2}\partial_{x}\bar{u}_{\tau},W)
and
({U}^{\sharp}_{\tau},\ell^{\sharp}_{\tau},W^{\sharp}_{\tau})
have the same law for each
\tau>0
.

Moreover,
U_{\epsilon}\in C(0,T;H^{2}(0,1))
,
\tilde{\mathbb{P}}
-almost surely.

Remark 3.1
.

Since every closed ball in
C(0,T;H^{2}(0,1))
is a measurable subset of
C(0,T;H^{1}(0,1))
, we also have that the limit
U_{\epsilon}\in C(0,T;H^{2}(0,1))
,
\tilde{\mathbb{P}}
-almost surely.

Remark 3.2
.

Let
\tilde{\mathcal{F}}_{t}^{\prime}
be the
\sigma
-algebra generated by the random variables
U_{\epsilon}(s),W^{\sharp}(s)
, for all
s\leq t
. Define

\mathcal{N}:=\{\mathcal{A}\in\tilde{\mathcal{F}}\ |\ \tilde{\mathbb{P}}(\mathcal{A})=0\},\qquad\tilde{\mathcal{F}}_{t}:=\cap_{s\geq t}\sigma(\tilde{\mathcal{F}}_{s}^{\prime}\cup\mathcal{N}).

Then,
(\tilde{\mathcal{F}}_{t})_{0\leq t\leq T}
defines a complete right continuous filtration for which
(U_{\epsilon},W^{\sharp})
is an adapted process. The construction of a complete right continuous filtration for
{U}_{\tau}^{\sharp}
(for each
\tau
) on the new probability space
(\tilde{\Omega},\tilde{\mathcal{F}},\tilde{\mathbb{P}})
, can be done in a similar fashion.

Next we proceed to identify the limit
U_{\epsilon}
in Theorem
3.3
, as a weak entropy solution to the system (
2.1
)–(
2.2
) in the sense of Theorem
2.1
, by passing to the limit in the entropy equality (
3.31
). Note however that the entropy equality (
3.31
) involves all of the approximations
{U}_{\tau}
,
\bar{U}_{\tau}
,
\tilde{U}_{\tau}
defined in (
2.16
), whereas we only passed
{U}_{\tau}
through Skorohod to obtain
{U}^{\sharp}_{\tau}
(see Theorem
3.3
). Therefore, on the new probability space
(\tilde{\Omega},\tilde{\mathcal{F}},\tilde{\mathbb{P}})
, we also define the other corresponding approximate solution forms:

(3.54)

\begin{split}\bar{U}_{\tau}^{\sharp}(t):=\textbf{S}(t-t_{n}){U}_{\tau}^{\sharp}(t_{n}),\qquad\tilde{U}_{\tau}^{\sharp}(t):=\textbf{R}(t,t_{n})\textbf{S}(\tau){U}_{\tau}^{\sharp}(t_{n}),\qquad\text{for }t\in(t_{n},t_{n+1}].\end{split}

Recall that by Theorem
3.3
,
{U}_{\tau}^{\sharp}
and
{U}_{\tau}
coincide in law in
C(0,T;H^{1}(0,1))
. Therefore,
\bar{U}_{\tau}^{\sharp},\tilde{U}_{\tau}^{\sharp}
coincide in law with
\bar{U}_{\tau},\tilde{U}_{\tau}
in
L^{\infty}(0,T;H^{1}(0,1))
. We make the following observation about the new approximate solutions (
3.54
), namely that they converge as
\tau\to 0
to the same limit
U_{\epsilon}
, obtained in Theorem
3.3
.

Proposition 3.6
.

As
\tau\to 0
,

\bar{U}^{\sharp}_{\tau}\to U_{\epsilon},\qquad\tilde{U}^{\sharp}_{\tau}\to U_{\epsilon},\qquad\text{in }L^{\infty}(0,T;H^{1}(0,1)),\quad\tilde{\mathbb{P}}\text{-almost surely},

where the limit
U_{\epsilon}
is previously defined in Theorem
3.3
. In addition, we also have the stronger convergence that
\bar{\rho}^{\sharp}_{\tau}\to\rho_{\epsilon}
in
C(0,T;H^{1}(0,1))
,
\tilde{\mathbb{P}}
-almost surely.

Proof.

This is a direct consequence of the fact that by Lemma
3.3
, Lemma
3.4
, and equivalence of laws in Theorem
3.3
, we have that :

(3.55)

\lim_{\tau\to 0}\mathbb{E}\|{U}_{\tau}^{\sharp}(t)-\bar{U}_{\tau}^{\sharp}(t)\|_{L^{\infty}(0,T;H^{1}(0,1))}^{2}=0,\qquad\lim_{\tau\to 0}\mathbb{E}\|{U}_{\tau}^{\sharp}(t)-\tilde{U}_{\tau}^{\sharp}(t)\|_{L^{\infty}(0,T;H^{1}(0,1))}^{2}=0.

Then, we obtain the desired result by the convergence of
{U}^{\sharp}_{\tau}\to U_{\epsilon}
in
C(0,T;H^{1}(0,1))
,
\tilde{\mathbb{P}}
-almost surely, due to Theorem
3.3
. The stronger convergence of
\bar{\rho}^{\sharp}_{\tau}\to\rho_{\epsilon}
in
C(0,T;H^{1}(0,1))
,
\tilde{\mathbb{P}}
-almost surely, follows from the fact that
\bar{\rho}_{\tau}
on the original probability space is continuous in time, by the definition in (
2.16
) and the fact that the stochastic subproblem does not change
\rho
(since the stochasticity is only in the momentum equation).
∎

We now use Theorem
3.3
and Proposition
3.6
to show the following almost everywhere convergence result, which will be important for the
\tau\to 0
limit passage of the approximate entropy formulation.

Proposition 3.7
.

For almost every
(\tilde{\omega},t,x)\in\tilde{\Omega}\times[0,T]\times[0,1]
:

U^{\sharp}_{\tau}(\tilde{\omega},t,x)\to U_{\epsilon}(\tilde{\omega},t,x),\qquad\bar{U}^{\sharp}_{\tau}(\tilde{\omega},t,x)\to U_{\epsilon}(\tilde{\omega},t,x),\qquad\tilde{U}^{\sharp}_{\tau}(\tilde{\omega},t,x)\to U_{\epsilon}(\tilde{\omega},t,x),

\partial_{x}U^{\sharp}_{\tau}(\tilde{\omega},t,x)\to\partial_{x}U_{\epsilon}(\tilde{\omega},t,x),\qquad\partial_{x}\bar{U}^{\sharp}_{\tau}(\tilde{\omega},t,x)\to\partial_{x}U_{\epsilon}(\tilde{\omega},t,x),\qquad\partial_{x}\tilde{U}^{\sharp}_{\tau}(\tilde{\omega},t,x)\to\partial_{x}U_{\epsilon}(\tilde{\omega},t,x).

Proof.

From Theorem
3.1
and equivalence of laws, we have that

\mathbb{E}\|U^{\sharp}_{\tau}(\tilde{\omega},t,x)\|_{C^{\alpha}(0,T;H^{1}(0,1))}^{2}\leq C,\qquad\text{ for }\alpha\in[0,1/4),

for a constant
C
that depends on
\alpha
,
\epsilon
, and
T
, but is independent of
\tau
. Therefore, for
i=0,1
:

\mathbb{E}\|\partial_{x}^{i}U^{\sharp}_{\tau}\|_{L^{1}((0,T)\times(0,1))}^{2}\leq C,

for a constant
C
that is independent of
\tau
. By Theorem
3.3
, we have that
U^{\sharp}_{\tau}\to U_{\epsilon}
,
\tilde{\mathbb{P}}
-almost surely, as
\tau\to 0
in
L^{\infty}(0,T;H^{1}(0,1))
, and hence for
i=0,1
, we have that
\partial_{x}^{i}U^{\sharp}_{\tau}\to\partial_{x}^{i}U_{\epsilon}
as
\tau\to 0
,
\tilde{\mathbb{P}}
-almost surely, in
L^{1}((0,T)\times(0,1))
. So by interpreting the approximate solutions
U^{\sharp}_{\tau}
as
L^{1}((0,T)\times(0,1))
valued random variables, we can apply the Vitali convergence theorem to deduce that
U^{\sharp}_{\tau}\to U_{\epsilon}
in
L^{1}(\tilde{\Omega}\times(0,T)\times(0,1))
as
\tau\to 0
, which implies the desired almost everywhere convergence. A similar argument completes the verification of the remainder of the convergences.
∎

Next, we show positivity of the limiting density
\rho_{\epsilon}
in
U_{\epsilon}:=(\rho_{\epsilon},m_{\epsilon})
, by using the convergence of the terms
\ell_{\tau}^{\sharp}\to\ell_{\epsilon}
in Theorem
3.3
corresponding to the quantities
\bar{\rho}_{\tau}^{1/2}\partial_{x}\bar{u}_{\tau}
, in the space
L^{2}((0,T)\times(0,1))
with the weak topology, which are needed to apply Proposition
3.3
.

Lemma 3.5

(Positivity of the limiting density)
.

We can identify the terms
\ell_{\tau}^{\sharp}
and
\ell_{\epsilon}
on the new probability space as:

\ell_{\tau}^{\sharp}=(\bar{\rho}^{\sharp}_{\tau})^{1/2}\partial_{x}\bar{u}_{\tau}^{\sharp},\qquad\ell_{\epsilon}=\rho_{\epsilon}^{1/2}\partial_{x}u_{\epsilon}.

Furthermore, for all
\tau>0
,

\min_{(t,x)\in[0,T]\times[0,1]}\bar{\rho}_{\tau}^{\sharp}(t,x,\tilde{\omega})\geq c(\tilde{\omega})>0,\qquad\min_{(t,x)\in[0,T]\times[0,1]}{\rho}_{\tau}^{\sharp}(t,x,\tilde{\omega})\geq c(\tilde{\omega})>0,

and

\min_{(t,x)\in[0,T]\times[0,1]}\rho_{\epsilon}(t,x,\tilde{\omega})\geq c(\tilde{\omega})>0\qquad

\tilde{\mathbb{P}}
-almost surely, for an almost surely positive random variable
c(\tilde{\omega})
that is independent of
\tau
.

Proof.

The fact that
\ell_{\tau}^{\sharp}=(\bar{\rho}_{\tau}^{\sharp})^{1/2}\partial_{x}\bar{u}_{\tau}^{\sharp}
follows by the equivalence of laws in Theorem
3.3
. Since by Theorem
3.3
,

(\bar{\rho}^{\sharp}_{\tau})^{1/2}\partial_{x}\bar{u}_{\tau}^{\sharp}\rightharpoonup\ell_{\epsilon},\quad\text{weakly in }L^{2}((0,T)\times(0,1)),\quad\tilde{\mathbb{P}}\text{-almost surely},

we conclude by the uniform boundedness principle, that there exists a positive random variable
C(\tilde{\omega})
independent of
\tau
, such that

(3.56)

\|(\bar{\rho}^{\sharp}_{\tau})^{1/2}\partial_{x}\bar{u}_{\tau}^{\sharp}\|_{L^{2}((0,T)\times(0,1))}=\int_{0}^{T}\int_{0}^{1}\bar{\rho}^{\sharp}_{\tau}(\partial_{x}\bar{u}_{\tau}^{\sharp})^{2}dxdt\leq C(\tilde{\omega}).

In addition, by the equivalence of laws in Theorem
3.3
and the uniform
L^{\infty}
bounds in Proposition
3.1
, we have that for
\bar{u}_{\tau}^{\sharp}:=\frac{\bar{m}_{\tau}^{\sharp}}{\bar{\rho}_{\tau}^{\sharp}}
, there exists a deterministic constant
C_{\epsilon}
that depends only on
\epsilon
, such that

(3.57)

\|\bar{u}^{\sharp}_{\tau}\|_{L^{\infty}((0,T)\times(0,1))}\leq C_{\epsilon},\qquad\tilde{\mathbb{P}}\text{-almost surely.}

Since
\bar{\rho}^{\sharp}_{\tau}
also satisfies the continuity equation (
3.30
) with
\bar{u}^{\sharp}_{\tau}
, we can hence apply Proposition
3.3
to conclude that

(3.58)

\bar{\rho}_{\tau}^{\sharp}\geq c_{min}(\tilde{\omega})>0,

for some almost surely positive random variable
c_{min}(\tilde{\omega})
that crucially is
independent of
\tau
. The fact that
c_{min}(\tilde{\omega})
is independent of
\tau
follows from Proposition
3.3
and the fact that the estimates (
3.56
) and (
3.57
) are independent of
\tau
. Moreover, since density is kept constant in the stochastic subproblem,
\tilde{\rho}_{\tau}^{\sharp}\geq c_{min}(\tilde{\omega})>0
, and thus,
\rho_{\tau}^{\sharp}
(which is a linear interpolation of
\bar{\rho}^{\sharp}_{\tau}
and
\tilde{\rho}^{\sharp}_{\tau}
by (
2.16
) and equivalence of laws) satisfies
{\rho}_{\tau}^{\sharp}\geq c_{min}(\tilde{\omega})>0
.

Hence, because
\bar{\rho}^{\sharp}_{\tau}\to\rho_{\epsilon}
in
C(0,T;H^{1}(0,1))
and hence in
C([0,T]\times[0,1])
also,
\tilde{\mathbb{P}}
-almost surely, we conclude from (
3.58
) that

\rho_{\epsilon}\geq c_{min}(\tilde{\omega})>0,

also, for the same
c_{min}(\tilde{\omega})
as in (
3.58
).

To identify the limit
\ell_{\epsilon}
as
\rho_{\epsilon}\partial_{x}u_{\epsilon}
, we will prove that the convergences in Theorem
3.3
imply that

(3.59)

\ell_{\tau}^{\sharp}=(\bar{\rho}_{\tau}^{\sharp})^{1/2}\partial_{x}\bar{u}_{\tau}^{\sharp}\rightharpoonup\rho_{\epsilon}^{1/2}\partial_{x}u_{\epsilon},\quad\text{ weakly in }L^{2}((0,T)\times(0,1)),\quad\tilde{\mathbb{P}}\text{-almost surely.}

It suffices to show (
3.59
) in order to identify the limit
\ell_{\epsilon}
in Theorem
3.3
as
\rho_{\epsilon}^{1/2}\partial_{x}u_{\epsilon}
, and hence conclude the proof, so we will focus on establishing the weak convergence in (
3.59
) for the remainder of the proof. By integration by parts, we will establish (
3.59
) once we show that for all
\varphi\in C_{c}^{\infty}([0,T]\times[0,1])
:

(3.60)

\int_{0}^{T}\int_{0}^{1}\frac{\bar{m}_{\tau}^{\sharp}\partial_{x}\bar{\rho}_{\tau}^{\sharp}}{(\bar{\rho}_{\tau}^{\sharp})^{3/2}}\varphi dxdt\to\int_{0}^{T}\int_{0}^{1}\frac{m_{\epsilon}\partial_{x}\rho_{\epsilon}}{\rho_{\epsilon}^{3/2}}\varphi dxdt,

(3.61)

\int_{0}^{T}\int_{0}^{1}\frac{\partial_{x}\bar{m}_{\tau}^{\sharp}}{(\bar{\rho}_{\tau}^{\sharp})^{1/2}}\varphi dxdt\to\int_{0}^{T}\int_{0}^{1}\frac{\partial_{x}m_{\epsilon}}{\rho_{\epsilon}^{1/2}}\varphi dxdt,

as
\tau\to 0
,
\tilde{\mathbb{P}}
-almost surely. We will now establish each of these convergences.

Proof of (
3.60
).
To see this, we note that uniformly in
\tau
:

(3.62)

\left|\int_{0}^{T}\int_{0}^{1}\frac{\bar{m}_{\tau}^{\sharp}}{(\bar{\rho}^{\sharp})^{3/2}}\varphi(\partial_{x}\rho_{\epsilon}-\partial_{x}\bar{\rho}_{\tau}^{\sharp})dxdt\right|\leq c(\tilde{\omega})\int_{0}^{T}\int_{0}^{1}|\partial_{x}\rho_{\epsilon}-\partial_{x}\bar{\rho}_{\tau}^{\sharp}|dxdt\leq c(\tilde{\omega})T\|\rho_{\epsilon}-\bar{\rho}_{\tau}^{\sharp}\|_{C(0,T;H^{1}(0,1))},

by the uniform lower bound in (
3.58
), and the fact that
\|\bar{m}_{\tau}^{\sharp}\|_{C([0,T]\times[0,1])}\leq C
independently of
\tau
by Proposition
3.1
and equivalence of laws. In addition, note that
\displaystyle\left|\frac{\bar{m}_{\tau}^{\sharp}}{(\bar{\rho}_{\tau}^{\sharp})^{3/2}}\right|\leq c(\tilde{\omega})
uniformly in
\tau
and
\displaystyle\frac{\bar{m}_{\tau}^{\sharp}}{(\bar{\rho}_{\tau}^{\sharp})^{3/2}}\to\frac{m_{\epsilon}}{\rho_{\epsilon}^{3/2}}
by the lower bound (
3.58
) and the convergences in
C([0,T]\times[0,1])
in Proposition
3.6
by Sobolev embedding. Hence, by the dominated convergence theorem,

(3.63)

\left|\int_{0}^{T}\int_{0}^{1}\left(\frac{m_{\epsilon}}{\rho_{\epsilon}^{3/2}}-\frac{\bar{m}_{\tau}^{\sharp}}{(\bar{\rho}_{\tau}^{\sharp})^{3/2}}\right)\varphi\partial_{x}\rho_{\epsilon}dxdt\right|\to 0,\qquad\text{ as }\tau\to 0,

\tilde{\mathbb{P}}
-almost surely. So by combining (
3.62
) and (
3.63
) and using Proposition
3.6
, we obtain the convergence (
3.60
).

Proof of (
3.61
).
Similarly, by the uniform lower bound in (
3.58
),

\left|\int_{0}^{T}\int_{0}^{1}\frac{1}{(\bar{\rho}_{\tau}^{\sharp})^{1/2}}\varphi(\partial_{x}m_{\epsilon}-\partial_{x}\bar{m}_{\tau}^{\sharp})\right|\leq C(\tilde{\omega})T\|m_{\epsilon}-\bar{m}_{\tau}^{\sharp}\|_{L^{\infty}(0,T;H^{1}(0,1))},

and similarly, by dominated convergence and the uniform lower bound in (
3.58
),

\left|\int_{0}^{T}\int_{0}^{1}\left(\frac{1}{\rho_{\epsilon}^{1/2}}-\frac{1}{(\bar{\rho}^{\sharp}_{\tau})^{1/2}}\right)\varphi(\partial_{x}m_{\epsilon})\right|\to 0,\qquad\text{ as }\tau\to 0.

The proof of (
3.61
) is thus completed by using Proposition
3.6
.
∎

Finally, before we carry out the limit passage as
\tau\to 0
in the approximate entropy balance equation (
3.31
), we note that this approximate entropy balance equation contains various nonlinear functions of the approximate solution
U_{\epsilon}
, defined via the entropy-flux pairs
(\eta,H)
. Thus, it will be useful to derive certain analytic bounds on these nonlinear functionals of
U_{\epsilon}
, as functions of the state variables
(\rho,m)
. These properties can be derived algebraically from the explicit formula for the entropy-flux pairs
(\eta,H)
given in (
1.8
).

Lemma 3.6
.

Let
(\eta,H)
be the entropy-flux pair defined via the relations (
1.8
) for functions convex
g\in C^{2}(\mathbb{R})
. Then, for all
(\rho,m)\in\mathbb{R}^{+}\times\mathbb{R}
satisfying

(3.64)

0<\rho\leq M,\qquad\left|\frac{m}{\rho}\right|\leq M

for some positive constant
M
, there exists a constant
C_{g,M}
depending only on the choice of the convex function
g\in C^{2}(\mathbb{R})
and the positive constant
M
, such that

|\eta(\rho,m)|\leq C_{g,M}\rho,\qquad|H(\rho,m)|\leq C_{g,M}\rho,

|\nabla_{\rho,m}\eta(\rho,m)|\leq C_{g,M},\qquad\rho|\nabla^{2}_{\rho,m}\eta(\rho,m)|\leq C_{g,M}.

Proof.

This result is proved in Proposition 1.3 in
[
48
]
, and it follows immediately by direct computations using the formulas in (
1.8
) along with the inequalities in (
3.64
). We refer the reader to
[
48
]
for the details of the proof.

∎

Now, we have all of the necessary components needed to pass to the limit as
\tau\to 0
in the approximate entropy balance equation (
3.31
). In particular, we proceed to show that the limit
U_{\epsilon}
satisfies the weak formulation of the parabolic system (
2.1
)–(
2.2
) and the desired entropy balance equation at the
\epsilon
level, stated in Theorem
2.1
.

Theorem 3.4

(Identification of the limit)
.

Suppose
U_{\epsilon 0}\in H^{2}(0,1)
and
\rho_{\epsilon 0}\geq c_{\epsilon}
for some (deterministic) constant
c_{\epsilon}>0
, and the noise coefficient
\sigma_{\epsilon}
satisfies (
1.14
).
Let
U_{\epsilon}
be defined as in Theorem
3.3
. Then, the following properties hold:

•

U_{\epsilon}
satisfies the following weak formulation for every
\varphi\in C_{c}^{2}((0,1))
:

(3.65)

\begin{split}\int_{0}^{1}U_{\epsilon}(t)\varphi(x)\,dx=&\int_{0}^{1}U_{\epsilon 0}\varphi(x)\,dx+\int_{0}^{t}\int_{0}^{1}F(U_{\epsilon})\partial_{x}\varphi\,dx\,ds-\int_{0}^{t}\int_{0}^{1}\begin{pmatrix}0\\
\alpha m_{\epsilon}\end{pmatrix}\varphi(x)\,dx\,ds\\
+&\epsilon\int_{0}^{t}\int_{0}^{1}U_{\epsilon}\partial_{x}^{2}\varphi+\int_{0}^{t}\int_{0}^{1}\begin{pmatrix}0\\
\sigma_{\epsilon}(x,U_{\epsilon})\end{pmatrix}\varphi(x)\,dx\,dW^{\sharp}(s),\;\;\;\tilde{\mathbb{P}}\text{-almost surely,}\\
\end{split}

where the function
F
is defined in (
1.5
).

•

More generally, for any entropy-flux pair
(\eta,H)
generated by a convex function
g\in C^{2}(\mathbb{R})
via the formulas (
1.8
),
U_{\epsilon}
satisfies the following entropy equality for every
\varphi\in C_{c}^{2}((0,1))
:

(3.66)

\begin{split}&\int_{0}^{1}\eta(U_{\epsilon}(t))\varphi(x)\,dx=\int_{0}^{1}\eta(U_{\epsilon 0})\varphi(x)\,dx+\int_{0}^{t}\int_{0}^{1}H(U_{\epsilon})\partial_{x}\varphi\,dx\,ds\\
&-\int_{0}^{t}\int_{0}^{1}m_{\epsilon}\partial_{m}\eta(U_{\epsilon})\varphi(x)\,dx\,ds+\epsilon\int_{0}^{t}\int_{0}^{1}\eta(U_{\epsilon})\partial_{x}^{2}\varphi\,dx\,ds+\epsilon\int_{0}^{t}\int_{0}^{1}\langle\nabla^{2}\eta(U_{\epsilon})\partial_{x}U_{\epsilon},\partial_{x}U_{\epsilon}\rangle\varphi(x)\,dx\,ds\\
+&\int_{0}^{t}\int_{0}^{1}\partial_{m}\eta(U_{\epsilon})\sigma_{\epsilon}(x,U_{\epsilon})\varphi(x)\,dx\,dW^{\sharp}(s)+\frac{1}{2}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{0}^{1}\partial_{m}^{2}\eta(U_{\epsilon})\sigma_{\epsilon}^{2}(x,U_{\epsilon})\varphi(x)\,dx\,ds,\end{split}

\tilde{\mathbb{P}}
-almost surely.

Proof.

Recall that
{U}_{\tau}(t)
satisfies the entropy balance equation (
3.31
). Then by the equivalence of laws between
{U}_{\tau}^{\sharp}(t)
, and the definition of
\bar{U}_{\tau}^{\sharp}
and
\tilde{U}_{\tau}^{\sharp}
in (
3.54
), the same approximate balance equation (
3.31
) holds for the random variables on the probability space
(\tilde{\Omega},\tilde{\mathcal{F}},\tilde{\mathbb{P}})
given by Theorem
3.3
. Below, we rewrite the equation (
3.31
) so that the numerical errors between the linear interpolant
{U}_{\tau}^{\sharp}
and the solutions to the two subproblems
\bar{U}_{\tau}^{\sharp}
and
\tilde{U}_{\tau}^{\sharp}
, see the definitions in (
2.16
), are reflected:

(3.67)

\begin{split}&\int_{0}^{1}\eta({U}_{\tau}^{\sharp}(t))\varphi(x)\,dx\\
=&\int_{0}^{1}\eta(U_{\epsilon 0})\varphi(x)\,dx+\int_{0}^{t}\int_{0}^{1}H({U}_{\tau}^{\sharp})\partial_{x}\varphi\,dx\,ds-\alpha\int_{0}^{t}\int_{0}^{1}{m}_{\tau}^{\sharp}\partial_{m}\eta({U}_{\tau}^{\sharp})\varphi(x)\,dx\,ds\\
+&\epsilon\int_{0}^{t}\int_{0}^{1}\eta({U}_{\tau}^{\sharp})\partial_{x}^{2}\varphi-\langle\nabla^{2}\eta({U}_{\tau}^{\sharp})\partial_{x}{U}_{\tau}^{\sharp},\partial_{x}{U}_{\tau}^{\sharp}\rangle\varphi(x)\,dx\,ds\\
+&\frac{1}{2}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{0}^{1}\sigma_{\epsilon}^{2}(x,\tilde{U}_{\tau}^{\sharp})\partial_{m}^{2}\eta({U}_{\tau}^{\sharp})\varphi(x)\,dx\,ds+\int_{0}^{t}\int_{0}^{1}\partial_{m}\eta({U}_{\tau}^{\sharp})\sigma_{\epsilon}(x,{U}_{\tau}^{\sharp})\varphi(x)\,dx\,dW^{\sharp}(s)\\
+&\int_{0}^{t}\int_{0}^{1}\left(H(\bar{U}_{\tau}^{\sharp})-H({U}_{\tau}^{\sharp})\right)\partial_{x}\varphi\,dx\,ds-\alpha\int_{0}^{t}\int_{0}^{1}\left(\bar{m}_{\tau}^{\sharp}\partial_{m}\eta(\bar{U}_{\tau}^{\sharp})-{m}_{\tau}^{\sharp}\partial_{m}\eta({U}_{\tau}^{\sharp})\right)\varphi(x)\,dx\,ds\\
+&\epsilon\int_{0}^{t}\int_{0}^{1}\left(\eta(\bar{U}_{\tau}^{\sharp})-\eta({U}_{\tau}^{\sharp})\right)\partial_{x}^{2}\varphi-\left(\langle\nabla^{2}\eta(\bar{U}_{\tau}^{\sharp})\partial_{x}\bar{U}_{\tau}^{\sharp},\partial_{x}\bar{U}_{\tau}^{\sharp}\rangle-\langle\nabla^{2}\eta({U}_{\tau}^{\sharp})\partial_{x}{U}_{\tau}^{\sharp},\partial_{x}{U}_{\tau}^{\sharp}\rangle\right)\varphi(x)\,dx\,ds\\
+&\frac{1}{2}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{0}^{1}\left(\partial_{m}^{2}\eta(\tilde{U}_{\tau}^{\sharp})\sigma_{\epsilon}^{2}(x,\tilde{U}_{\tau}^{\sharp})-\partial_{m}^{2}\eta({U}_{\tau}^{\sharp})\sigma_{\epsilon}^{2}(x,{U}_{\tau}^{\sharp})\right)\varphi(x)\,dx\,ds\\
+&\int_{0}^{t}\int_{0}^{1}\left(\partial_{m}\eta(\tilde{U}_{\tau}^{\sharp})\sigma_{\epsilon}(x,\tilde{U}_{\tau}^{\sharp})-\partial_{m}\eta({U}_{\tau}^{\sharp})\sigma_{\epsilon}(x,{U}_{\tau}^{\sharp})\right)\varphi(x)\,dx\,dW^{\sharp}(s)\\
+&\frac{t-t_{n}}{\tau}\left[\int_{t}^{t_{n+1}}\int_{0}^{1}\left(H(\bar{U}_{\tau}^{\sharp})\partial_{x}\varphi-\alpha\bar{m}_{\tau}^{\sharp}\partial_{m}\eta(\bar{U}_{\tau}^{\sharp})\varphi(x)+\epsilon\eta(\bar{U}_{\tau}^{\sharp})\partial_{x}^{2}\varphi-\epsilon\langle\nabla^{2}\eta(\bar{U}_{\tau}^{\sharp})\partial_{x}\bar{U}_{\tau}^{\sharp},\partial_{x}\bar{U}_{\tau}^{\sharp}\rangle\varphi(x)\right)\,dx\,ds\right]\\
+&\frac{t-t_{n}}{\tau}\left[\frac{1}{2}\int_{t_{n}}^{t}\int_{0}^{1}\partial_{m}^{2}\eta(\tilde{U}_{\tau}^{\sharp})\sigma_{\epsilon}^{2}(x,\tilde{U}_{\tau}^{\sharp})\varphi(x)\,dx\,ds+\int_{t_{n}}^{t}\int_{0}^{1}\partial_{m}\eta(\tilde{U}_{\tau}^{\sharp})\sigma_{\epsilon}(x,\tilde{U}_{\tau}^{\sharp})\varphi(x)\,dx\,dW^{\sharp}(s)\right]\\
+&\int_{0}^{1}\mathrm{R}_{2}(\bar{U}_{\tau}^{\sharp}-\tilde{U}_{\tau}^{\sharp})\varphi(x)\,dx\,ds\\
=:&\int_{0}^{1}\eta(U_{\epsilon 0})\varphi(x)\,dx+\int_{0}^{t}\int_{0}^{1}H({U}_{\tau}^{\sharp})\partial_{x}\varphi\,dx\,ds-\alpha\int_{0}^{t}\int_{0}^{1}{m}_{\tau}^{\sharp}\partial_{m}\eta({U}_{\tau}^{\sharp})\varphi(x)\,dx\,ds\\
+&\epsilon\int_{0}^{t}\int_{0}^{1}\eta({U}_{\tau}^{\sharp})\partial_{x}^{2}\varphi-\langle\nabla^{2}\eta({U}_{\tau}^{\sharp})\partial_{x}{U}_{\tau}^{\sharp},\partial_{x}{U}_{\tau}^{\sharp}\rangle\varphi(x)\,dx\,ds\\
+&\frac{1}{2}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{0}^{1}\partial_{m}^{2}\eta({U}_{\tau}^{\sharp})\sigma_{\epsilon}^{2}(x,{U}_{\tau}^{\sharp})\varphi(x)\,dx\,ds+\int_{0}^{t}\int_{0}^{1}\partial_{m}\eta({U}_{\tau}^{\sharp})\sigma_{\epsilon}(x,{U}_{\tau}^{\sharp})\varphi(x)\,dx\,dW^{\sharp}(s)\\
&+\sum_{i=1}^{10}E_{i}(t),\end{split}

where
\mathrm{R}_{2}(\bar{U}_{\tau}^{\sharp}-\tilde{U}_{\tau}^{\sharp})
is the second order Taylor remainder term given by

\mathrm{R}_{2}(\bar{U}_{\tau}^{\sharp}-\tilde{U}_{\tau}^{\sharp})=\frac{1}{2}(\tilde{U}_{\tau}^{\sharp}-\bar{U}_{\tau}^{\sharp})^{T}\left((\frac{t-t_{n}}{\tau})^{2}\nabla^{2}\eta(\mathbf{\xi}_{1}^{\sharp})-\frac{t-t_{n}}{\tau}\nabla^{2}\eta(\mathbf{\xi}_{2}^{\sharp})\right)(\tilde{U}_{\tau}^{\sharp}-\bar{U}_{\tau}^{\sharp}),

where
\xi_{1}^{\sharp}=\xi_{1}^{\sharp}(t,x),\xi_{2}^{\sharp}=\xi_{2}^{\sharp}(t,x)\in\mathbb{R}^{2}
are two points on the line segment joining
\bar{U}_{\tau}^{\sharp}(t,x)
and
\tilde{U}_{\tau}^{\sharp}(t,x)
, for every
(t,x)\in Q_{T}
(see calculation in (
3.34
)-(
3.35
)).
We remark that the error terms
\{E_{i}\}_{i=1}^{6}
arise due to the numerical error between
{U}_{\tau}^{\sharp}
,
\bar{U}_{\tau}^{\sharp}
, and
\tilde{U}_{\tau}^{\sharp}
as defined in (
2.16
), and the error terms
\{E_{i}\}_{i=7}^{10}
are terms that are already in the entropy equality (
3.31
), which, we recall, arise from calculating the Taylor expansion of
\eta({U}_{\tau}^{\sharp})
, see (
3.35
). Our goal is to show that as
\tau\to 0
,
E_{i}\to 0
,
\tilde{\mathbb{P}}
-almost surely, as this will allow us to recover the entropy equality (
2.10
) as we pass
\tau\to 0
. For convenience, we collect all of the uniform bounds in
\tau
and convergences that we have altogether here. By equivalence of laws, from Proposition
3.1
and Lemma
3.5
, we have that for the approximate solutions and the fluid velocity
u
:

(3.68)

\|U^{\sharp}_{\tau}\|_{L^{\infty}(Q_{T})}\leq C,\qquad\|\bar{U}^{\sharp}_{\tau}\|_{L^{\infty}(Q_{T})}\leq C,\qquad\|\tilde{U}_{\tau}^{\sharp}\|_{L^{\infty(Q_{T})}}\leq C,

(3.69)

\|u^{\sharp}_{\tau}\|_{L^{\infty}(Q_{T})}\leq C,\qquad\|\bar{u}^{\sharp}_{\tau}\|_{L^{\infty}(Q_{T})}\leq C,\qquad\|\tilde{u}_{\tau}^{\sharp}\|_{L^{\infty(Q_{T})}}\leq C,

for a deterministic constant
C
,
\tilde{\mathbb{P}}
-almost surely, and

(3.70)

0<c(\tilde{\omega})\leq\rho^{\sharp}_{\tau}\leq C,

for a positive constant
c(\tilde{\omega})
depending on the outcome,
\tilde{\mathbb{P}}
-almost surely, but independent of
\tau
. Furthermore, from Proposition
3.3
and Proposition
3.6
, we have the
\tilde{\mathbb{P}}
-almost sure convergences:

(3.71)

U^{\sharp}_{\tau}\to U_{\epsilon},\qquad\bar{U}^{\sharp}_{\tau}\to U_{\epsilon},\qquad\tilde{U}^{\sharp}_{\tau}\to U_{\epsilon},\qquad\text{ in }L^{\infty}(0,T;H^{1}(0,1)).

Using these bounds and convergences, we now proceed to estimate the error terms, as follows.

Estimates of
E_{1}
,
E_{2}
, and
E_{3}
.

By (
3.68
), (
3.69
), and the bound on
H(\rho,m)
in Lemma
3.6
, we have that for some deterministic constant
C
,
|H(\bar{U}^{\sharp}_{\tau})-H(U^{\sharp}_{\tau})|\leq C
,
\tilde{\mathbb{P}}
-almost surely. Hence, since
\bar{U}^{\sharp}_{\tau}-U^{\sharp}_{\tau}\to 0
in
L^{\infty}(Q_{T})
,
\tilde{\mathbb{P}}
-almost surely using (
3.71
), and since
H(\rho,m)
is continuous in
(\rho,m)
away from vacuum, we have by dominated convergence that

|E_{1}|:=\left|\int_{0}^{t}\int_{0}^{1}\left(H(\bar{U}_{\tau}^{\sharp})-H({U}_{\tau}^{\sharp})\right)\partial_{x}\varphi\,dx\,ds\right|\to 0,

\tilde{\mathbb{P}}
-almost surely. We can also show that
E_{2}
and
E_{3}
converge to 0,
\tilde{\mathbb{P}}
-almost surely as
\tau\to 0
by a similar argument using the dominated convergence theorem.

Estimate of
E_{4}
.
Next, we estimate
E_{4}
:
